1. SYSTEM DESCRIPTION (what this app actually is)
ADTU ITMS is a Next.js 16 (src/proxy.ts = Next-16 middleware convention, package.json:99) university bus-transport management system with four personas — admin | moderator | driver | student (src/lib/user-service.ts:10) — plus a standalone WebSocket server (server/index.ts, ports 3001/9090) load-balanced by nginx (nginx/nginx.conf:47-70).
Identity. Firebase Auth is the authentication provider only (Google sign-in, src/lib/user-service.ts, src/contexts/auth-context.tsx:3). Firebase Firestore is not the system of record for users. Roles are resolved from PostgreSQL by src/lib/security/role-cache.ts ("No Firestore fallback. No dual-read"), consumed by withSecurity (src/lib/security/api-security.ts) and by the WS authenticateSocket (server/authenticator.ts:96-110, which prefers users.role in PG over the Firebase custom claim, 90s token cache).
Business core. (a) Applications & renewals — a state machine in src/lib/types/application.ts:24-31+ (noDoc → draft → awaiting_verification → verified → submitted → approved/rejected → verified_upcoming/pending_seat_allocation), orchestrated by src/domains/application/services/application.service.ts against PostgreSQL RPCs (approve_renewal_with_seat, identity_activate_student, …). (b) Capacity/seats — per-bus morning_load/evening_load vs capacity, gated by the feature flag SEAT_RELEASE_AT_SOFT_BLOCK (src/lib/config/capacity-flags.ts, default OFF, unset in .env). (c) Payments — Razorpay (create-order → verify-payment → HMAC webhook at src/app/api/payment/webhook/razorpay/route.ts) plus an offline ledger in payments-supabase.ts; actual persisted statuses are Pending|Completed|Rejected. (d) Trips/GPS — driver starts a trip (/api/driver/initiate-trip), posts GPS every 2 s (src/app/driver/live-tracking/page.tsx:1096 → /api/location/update), pipeline enforces bounds/accuracy/jump/speed/replay plus an active-trip check and a Redis Lua guard (src/domains/gps/services/gps-pipeline.service.ts:142-230), then broadcasts bus_location_update. (e) Waiting flags — student raises a flag at a stop; driver acks/boards; fan-out is WS-only. (f) Lifecycle — entitlement = status==='active' AND now < softBlock (src/lib/entitlement/transport-entitlement.ts), enforced server-side by requireTransportEntitlement on 3 routes and client-side by TransportEntitlementGuard; crons soft-block/hard-delete from pre-stored dates.
Access patterns. Four coexisting auth styles; rate limiting is an in-process Map; edge CSRF/rate/origin checks live in src/proxy.ts; CRON_SECRET, AM_WEBHOOK_SECRET, FIRST_ADMIN_BOOTSTRAP_SECRET, RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET are all fail-closed and timing-safe.

Feedback	Firestore feedbacks src/lib/feedback-utils.ts:33-234	—
Driver profile-change requests	Firestore profile_update_requests (3 routes + cleanup-helpers.ts:155)	—
Bus issues	Firestore bus_issues src/app/api/report-bus-issue/route.ts:70	—
Auth	Firebase Auth	custom claim = degraded fallback
Key structural fact: there are exactly two anon SELECT policies in the whole schema (active_trips, bus_locations), the browser client is anon-only with no supabase.auth.* session anywhere in src/ (src/lib/supabase-client.ts:14-17), and all server writes use SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY (src/lib/supabase-server.ts). RLS TO authenticated policies are unreachable by this application — so every direct browser query resolves to fully open (2 tables) or fully closed (everything else).

4. FINDINGS
ROOT = C:\Users\ADMIN\Desktop\Projects\ITMS
FINDING A-01 | AUTHZ | HIGH | HIGH | CLASSIFICATION: BROKEN-ACCESS-CONTROL
The WebSocket location_update handler auto-binds an attacker-chosen busId to the session with no ownership validation, and that session.busId is the only key the subscribe handler uses to authorize waiting_flags_* / driver_wait_request_* channels. A driver can therefore read another bus's live waiting-flag stream (student names, stop names/coords) without ever passing the presence ownership check that exists for exactly this purpose.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\server\socket-router.ts:241-257 (const busId = session.busId || claimedBusId; … if (!session.busId && claimedBusId) sessionManager.setBusId(...)) · ROOT\server\socket-router.ts:114-131 (subscribe authorizes iff channelBusId === session.busId) · ROOT\server\socket-router.ts:177-200 (real ownership check lives only in presence) · ROOT\server\socket-router.ts:229 (only a role !== 'driver' check) · payload carries PII: ROOT\src\app\api\student\waiting-flag\route.ts:109 (emitEvent('waiting_flags_${busId}', 'waiting_flag_created', insertData)).
CONDITION TO CONFIRM: message ordering is per-socket and sequential (socket-router.ts:37-62), so location_update → subscribe executes in order. Not runtime-verified.
FINDING A-02 | SECURITY | HIGH | HIGH | CLASSIFICATION: UNAUTHENTICATED-DATA-EXPOSURE
active_trips and bus_locations are SELECT-able by role anon with USING (true) / status='active', and the browser holds only the public ANON key with no Supabase Auth session. Any visitor can read every vehicle's live GPS position and every active trip straight from PostgREST using the URL+key shipped in the JS bundle, completely bypassing the entitlement gate that HTTP routes enforce.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\supabase\COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:2551-2552 (active_trips_select_anon … USING (status = 'active')) · ROOT\supabase\COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:2559-2560 (bus_locations_select_anon … USING (true)) · ROOT\src\lib\supabase-client.ts:3,14-17 (anon key, no session) · zero supabase.auth.* occurrences in ROOT\src · entitlement is enforced only at ROOT\src\lib\entitlement\require-transport-entitlement.ts:36-49.
FINDING A-03 | FUNCTIONAL | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | CLASSIFICATION: BROKEN-READ-PATH / SCHEMA-DRIFT
The browser-side "initial snapshot" of waiting flags can never return data: waiting_flags has no anon SELECT policy, so useWaitingFlags silently receives 0 rows on every load/reconnect and the UI only ever shows flags that arrive later over WS. Independently, DynamicStudentMap selects five columns that do not exist in waiting_flags (lat, lng, accuracy, message, timestamp) → PostgREST 400, swallowed at console.warn. The two call sites also disagree on status (`

FINDING A-04 | DATA | MEDIUM | HIGH | CLASSIFICATION: SILENT-DATA-LOSS (UI)
Three admin/moderator Excel exporters read student_profiles / buses / routes / driver_profiles through the anon browser client instead of an authenticated API. Those tables have RLS enabled with no anon policy, so PostgREST returns 0 rows without an error; the exporters then build and download a workbook from rawData || [] → a valid, empty export with no warning. The moderator counterpart has a fallback; these three do not.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\src\app\admin\students\page.tsx:433-454 (handleExportStudents, query at :437-442, consumed at :454) · ROOT\src\app\admin\buses\page.tsx:226-246 (queries at :232-241) · ROOT\src\app\admin\routes\page.tsx:312-319 · ROOT\supabase\COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:2466-2474 (ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY on users/student_profiles/driver_profiles/buses/routes) · anon policy list contains only active_trips and bus_locations (:2551, :2559).
FINDING A-05 | SECURITY | HIGH | HIGH | CLASSIFICATION: UNCOMMITTED REGRESSION / TEST-BREAKING
An uncommitted edit to getClientIp flips X-Forwarded-For parsing from rightmost to leftmost. This (a) directly contradicts the module's own documented invariant INV-AUTH-006 and will fail its unit test, (b) diverges from src/proxy.ts, which still reads the rightmost entry, so the edge proxy and the API rate limiter can disagree about who the client is on the same request, and (c) behind this repo's own nginx, which appends the real peer with $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for, the leftmost entry is client-supplied — making the per-IP rate-limit key spoofable.
EVIDENCE (working tree, git diff): ROOT\src\lib\security\api-security.ts:124-131 (return parts[0]; + comment) vs HEAD behaviour parts[parts.length - 1] · ROOT\src\lib\security\__tests__\security-boundaries-master.test.ts:170-177 (expects 203.0.113.99 = rightmost for XFF 198.51.100.1, 203.0.113.99) · invariant name at :159 · ROOT\src\proxy.ts:175-179 (return parts[parts.length - 1];, unmodified) · ROOT\nginx\nginx.conf:100 (proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for) · consumed as rate-limit key at ROOT\src\lib\security\api-security.ts:244.
MITIGATING: both functions prefer x-real-ip/cf-connecting-ip first (api-security.ts:121, proxy.ts:172), and nginx does set X-Real-IP (nginx.conf:97), so under the nginx topology the XFF branch is not reached.
FINDING A-06 | INTEGRITY | HIGH | MEDIUM | CLASSIFICATION: UNCOMMITTED SCHEMA/CODE COUPLING
The working tree replaces a two-RPC sequence (bus_increment_capacity → identity_activate_student, with a compensating decrement) with a single fused RPC identity_activate_student_with_capacity. That function exists only in the uncommitted supabase/COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql. If the TS change ships without the DDL being applied to the target database, fresh-application approval fails at db.rpc(...) with "function does not exist". There is no migration file for it — only the edited snapshot.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\src\domains\application\services\application.service.ts:456-500 (diff hunk: db.rpc('identity_activate_student_with_capacity', …)) · ROOT\supabase\COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:744-832 (new CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.identity_activate_student_with_capacity + REVOKE EXECUTE … FROM public/authenticated/anon at :831-833) · git diff --stat shows both files modified and uncommitted · no matching file under ROOT\supabase\migrations (schema is delivered as a single COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql snapshot; package.json:59-60 runs supabase migration up).
FINDING A-07 | AUTHZ | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | CLASSIFICATION: INCOMPLETE-ENFORCEMENT
Transport entitlement is enforced on the HTTP student surface but never on the realtime surface. The WS presence handler authorizes a bus purely by comparing student_profiles.bus_id, and subscribe only re-checks that session.busId matches the channel — neither consults status, softBlock, or getTransportEntitlement. A soft_blocked / expired / suspended / pending_deletion student whose bus_id is still populated retains full waiting-flag and live-location access over WS, while the equivalent HTTP route returns 403.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\server\socket-router.ts:177-183 (student branch: if (data?.bus_id === claimedBusId) authorized = true; — no status predicate) · ROOT\server\socket-router.ts:114-131 (subscribe: bus-id equality only) · ROOT\src\lib\entitlement\require-transport-entitlement.ts:36-49 (HTTP equivalent, returns 403) · ROOT\src\lib\entitlement\transport-entitlement.ts:104-153 (canonical rule) · contrast ROOT\src\app\api\student\waiting-flag\route.ts:44 and ROOT\src\app\api\student\trip-status\route.ts:1.
UNCONFIRMED PRECONDITION: whether bus_id is nulled at soft-block — not established from source (see UNVERIFIED U-03).
FINDING A-08 | OBSERVABILITY | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | CLASSIFICATION: INCONSISTENT-EXPOSURE
The WS server's Prometheus endpoint is explicitly locked to loopback/RFC1918 in nginx, but the Next.js app's own /api/metrics — same Prometheus text format, plus a live Redis INFO probe — falls through the unrestricted location / and is reachable by anyone on the internet. /api/metrics/json on the same codebase does require auth, so the two exporters of the same registry disagree.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\nginx\nginx.conf:121-133 (location /metrics → allow 127.0.0.1; allow 10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16; deny all;) · ROOT\nginx\nginx.conf:104-117 (same for /health) · no location /api restriction anywhere in nginx.conf · ROOT\src\app\api\metrics\route.ts:6-9 (only NextResponse + metrics imports; no auth) and :15-40 (Redis INFO memory/stats probe) · by contrast ROOT\src\app\api\metrics\json\route.ts is verifyApiAuth.
FINDING A-09 | SECURITY | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | CLASSIFICATION: WEAK-RATE-LIMITING
All API rate limits are a process-local Map capped at 10 000 entries with oldest-key eviction. Consequences: limits do not apply across replicas (nginx balances across nextjs_backend), a 10k-key flood evicts existing counters (bypass), and every restart resets all counters — while the repo already runs Redis for other purposes. Several sensitive routes depend solely on this (upload, delete-image, create-first-admin use hand-rolled variants of the same in-memory primitive).
EVIDENCE: ROOT\src\lib\security\rate-limiter.ts:19-40 (class RateLimitCache, maxSize = 10000, this.cache.delete(firstKey) eviction) · :79-117 (checkRateLimit) · :167-186 (RateLimits table) · consumer ROOT\src\lib\security\api-security.ts:244 · ROOT\nginx\nginx.conf:56-64 (upstream nextjs_backend … least_conn) · hand-rolled equivalents: ROOT\src\app\api\upload\route.ts:47-51, ROOT\src\app\api\delete-image\route.ts:31-35, ROOT\src\app\api\create-first-admin\route.ts:9-16.
FINDING A-10 | CONFIG | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | CLASSIFICATION: FEATURE-OFF / SELF-HEALING-DISABLED
SEAT_RELEASE_AT_SOFT_BLOCK is absent from .env, so the seat-release-at-soft-block architecture is OFF in this workspace (legacy semantics: seats released only at hard/manual delete). Because integrity-sweep gates its self-healing adminReconcileBusLoads on that same flag, the compensating reconciliation never runs either — so the only automated repair net for capacity drift is disabled together with the feature it protects.
EVIDENCE: .env key inventory (52 keys, no SEAT_RELEASE_AT_SOFT_BLOCK) at ROOT\.env · ROOT\src\lib\config\capacity-flags.ts:39-41 (process.env.SEAT_RELEASE_AT_SOFT_BLOCK === 'true', header at :35-37 states "Default: OFF") · ROOT\src\app\api\cron\integrity-sweep\route.ts:21,39 (reconciliation gated) · ROOT\vercel.json schedules integrity-sweep daily 17 3 * * *.
FINDING A-11 | CONFIG | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | CLASSIFICATION: MALFORMED-ENV-ENTRY
ROOT\.env contains one line with no KEY= separator — a bare secret-shaped token sitting between DOCUMENT_PRIVATE_KEY and DOCUMENT_PUBLIC_KEY, i.e. a key name that was lost. Any code reading the intended variable will see undefined. .env is correctly untracked (git ls-files shows only .env.example), and .gitignore covers .env* with !.env.example.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\.env (52 entries enumerated; line in question immediately after DOCUMENT_PRIVATE_KEY) · ROOT\.gitignore:11-22 and :118-120 (.env* + !.env.example) · git ls-files | Select-String '^\.env' → .env.example only.
FINDING A-12 | OBSERVABILITY | LOW | HIGH | CLASSIFICATION: MISLEADING-METRIC
The deprecated WS location_update handler no longer processes or broadcasts anything, but still increments the gpsAccepted counter, so the metric counts frames that were deliberately ignored. No client sends this message type anywhere, so today the counter is either 0 or, in a legacy-client event, a false positive.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\server\socket-router.ts:272-279 (comment: "We no longer broadcast or cache from this handler" … metricsService.inc('gpsAccepted')) · grep for location_update across ROOT\src, ROOT\e2e, ROOT\scripts returns only bus_location_update event names, never the WS message type.

41 files under docs/ (~6 400 lines, the entire documentation set: architecture, evidence ledger, production gate, security decision log, GPS forensic report) are deleted in the working tree with no commit, alongside 4 modified source files and the schema snapshot. docs/ no longer exists on disk (Test-Path = False). A careless git add -A && git commit would irreversibly drop the audit/evidence base along with the in-flight code changes.

FINDING A-14 | DATA | LOW | MEDIUM | CLASSIFICATION: TYPE/SOURCE-OF-TRUTH MISMATCH
PaymentDatabaseStatus includes 'Failed', which no writer emits; the ledger only ever writes Pending, Completed, Rejected. Any exhaustive switch over this type compiled against the declared union will hit a case that cannot occur, and an operator filtering on Failed sees nothing.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\src\lib\payment\payment-state.ts:34 ('Pending' | 'Completed' | 'Failed') · persisted values in ROOT\src\lib\services\payments-supabase.ts ('Pending' | 'Completed' | 'Rejected') · consumer ROOT\src\app\api\payments\approve\route.ts:23-34 (WHERE status='Pending').
FINDING A-15 | CODE-DEFECT | LOW | HIGH | CLASSIFICATION: NO-OP-EXPRESSION
simulationConfig.syncSessionWithSimulatedDate || true is always truthy, so the intended toggle has no effect; combined with isExpired = simulationConfig?.enabled ? true : … immediately above, enabling simulation forces every student through the block path regardless of the stored date.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\src\lib\utils\renewal-utils.ts:104-108.
FINDING A-16 | OPS | LOW | HIGH | CLASSIFICATION: DOC/SCHEDULE-DRIFT
cleanup-stale-locks documents itself as a per-minute worker ("Should be called every minute via Vercel Cron") but is scheduled once daily at 04:00; a trip abandoned after the 600 s heartbeat timeout can therefore stay status='active' for up to ~24 h, during which /api/student/trip-status and the GPS active-trip check keep treating it as live.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\src\app\api\cron\cleanup-stale-locks\route.ts:13,17-18 (HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 600, "every minute") · ROOT\vercel.json:27-30 ("schedule": "0 4 * * *") · liveness filter ROOT\src\app\api\location\update\route.ts:95-107 (expires_at extended +600 s per throttled heartbeat).
FINDING A-17 | DEAD-LOGIC | LOW | HIGH | CLASSIFICATION: DEAD-ABSTRACTION
The socket middleware chain has no registered middleware — use() is never called — so runMiddlewareChain always returns true and the proceed gate in routeMessage is vestigial. Any future author adding authorization "the middleware way" would silently ship nothing.
EVIDENCE: ROOT\server\socket-middleware.ts:8-25 (only definition, no registration site; grep for use( / socket-middleware across ROOT\server returns just socket-router.ts:5) · ROOT\server\socket-router.ts:44-45.

1. AUTH BOUNDARY MATRIX
1.1 Auth model as implemented (verified from code)
Layer	What it actually does	Evidence
Edge proxy (src/proxy.ts)	IP rate limit (300/min), scanner-path block, CSRF/origin validation for all state-changing methods (exempts /webhook/, /cron/ paths, and requests carrying a Bearer/x-internal-token), security headers. It does NOT authenticate or authorize API calls — it only hints (X-Proxy-Auth: none)	proxy.ts:244-286, 309-394, 380-391
Per-route guards	All authorization lives here: withSecurity (auth + roles + rate limit + Zod + own CSRF check), verifyApiAuth(req, roles), verifyTokenOnly, inline adminAuth.verifyIdToken + resolveUserRole, requireModeratorPermission/requireAdminPermission, verifyCronAuth, validateStudentScannerContext	api-auth.ts:57-156, api-security.ts:236-395, cron-auth.ts, moderator-permissions.ts

Consequence: the security of every boundary is decided per route. The proxy's header comment claims "authentication verification + RBAC" (proxy.ts:8-9) but the code explicitly does not block (proxy.ts:386-390). All 180 routes were therefore audited individually.

2. FINDINGS
IDOR-01 — POST /api/applications/submit allows forcing another user's application state
- Severity: Medium · Confidence: High (code-proven) · Classification: Broken Object Level Authorization (BOLA/IDOR)
- Location: src/app/api/applications/submit/route.ts:16,26 → src/domains/application/services/application.service.ts:133-143 → supabase/COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:989-1023
- Preconditions: any cryptographically valid Firebase ID token (no role required, route.ts:12); target application in state verified.
- Description: the route takes a client-supplied applicationId and passes it straight to submit(applicationId, uid); submit() invokes RPC transition_application_state with p_actor_uid but performs no applicantUid comparison (contrast saveDraft, which correctly returns Unauthorized at application.service.ts:92-94). The SECURITY DEFINER SQL function (lines 1005-1022) loads the row by application_id only and updates it — the p_actor_uid is written into state_history/submitted_by, never checked. The state machine limits the reachable transition to verified → submitted.
- Impact: any authenticated student (or any logged-in account) can (a) forcibly submit any other student's verified application, (b) corrupt submitted_by / state_history attribution (audit integrity), (c) interfere with deadline-driven processing of a victim's application. No data is read; no privilege/role escalation.
- Compensating controls: narrow transition matrix (only verified → submitted); ownership enforced on save-draft and submit-final; rate limiting on the route.
- Recommendation: in submit(), fetch the application first and return Unauthorized when existing.applicantUid !== uid; additionally add IF v_app.applicant_uid <> p_actor_uid THEN RETURN jsonb(... 'status',403) inside the SQL function so every caller is protected.
RBAC-01 — GET /api/students returns full student PII to moderators without students.canView
- Severity: High · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken Function Level Authorization (missing permission check)
- Location: src/app/api/students/route.ts:19 (verifyApiAuth(request,['admin','moderator','driver'])), :112 (isStaff incl. moderator), :114-138
- Description: no requireModeratorPermission(auth,'students','canView') anywhere in the file. Because moderators/add creates moderators with every permission false (moderators/add/route.ts:25), a moderator whose students.canView was explicitly revoked (or never granted, or whose status is suspended → ZERO_MODERATOR_PERMISSIONS) still enumerates the entire student_profiles table, and as "staff" receives email, phone, alt phone, DOB, parent name, parent phone (route.ts:118-127). Drivers (also un-gated for permission) receive the reduced but still identifying set (name, enrollment, gender, faculty, bus, route, status).
- Impact: bulk PII disclosure + complete bypass of the students.canView control that guards GET /api/students/[id].
- Compensating controls: auth required; rate-limited (RateLimits.READ); no write capability.
- Recommendation: add requireModeratorPermission(auth,'students','canView') for role==='moderator', and restrict driver to buses assigned to them.
RBAC-02 — Driver list endpoints missing drivers.canView
- Severity: Medium · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken Function Level Authorization
- Location: src/app/api/drivers/route.ts:12 (verifyApiAuth(request,['admin','moderator']), no permission) · src/app/api/drivers/get-all/route.ts (requiredRoles:['admin','moderator'], returns email, phone, employeeId, busId, routeId)
- Description/Impact: a moderator with drivers.canView=false (the default for new moderators) enumerates all active drivers with contact PII, while GET /api/drivers/[id] correctly denies the same moderator.
- Recommendation: gate both with requireModeratorPermission(auth,'drivers','canView').
RBAC-03 — Application list / unauth-application detail missing applications.canView
- Severity: Medium · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken Function Level Authorization
- Location: src/app/api/applications/all/route.ts:16-21 · src/app/api/applications/get-unauth/[id]/route.ts:17-23 (both: verifyIdToken + role !== 'admin' && role !== 'moderator' only)
- Impact: moderators without applications.canView list all applications / read any unauth application record (application form data PII), while GET /api/applications/[id] enforces applications.canView (applications/[id]/route.ts:18).
- Recommendation: add requireModeratorPermission(auth,'applications','canView').
RBAC-04 — Bus create/update/list bypass buses.canAdd / buses.canEdit / buses.canView
- Severity: Medium · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken Function Level Authorization (inconsistent enforcement)
- Location: buses/create/route.ts:25-27 (role only) · buses/update/route.ts:26-28 (role only) · buses/route.ts:12 (GET: any authenticated) and :36 (POST role only) — versus buses/[id]/route.ts:17,39 which does call requireModeratorPermission(auth,'buses','canView'|'canEdit').
- Impact: a moderator with all bus permissions false can create and modify fleet records (capacity, driver assignment, route binding) and read the full bus inventory; students/drivers also read the full inventory.
- Recommendation: apply requireModeratorPermission(auth,'buses','canAdd'|'canEdit'|'canView') to the three routes (list may intentionally stay open to authenticated applicants — decide and document).
RBAC-05 — POST /api/routes missing routes.canAdd
- Severity: Medium · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken Function Level Authorization
- Location: src/app/api/routes/route.ts:35 (verifyApiAuth(request,['admin','moderator']), no permission) — versus routes/[id]/update/route.ts:12 (routes.canEdit) and routes/[id]/delete/route.ts:12 (routes.canDelete).
- Impact: any moderator (default all-false) can create routes with arbitrary stops; routes.canAdd is never enforced anywhere.
- Recommendation: add requireModeratorPermission(auth,'routes','canAdd').
RBAC-06 — POST /api/admin/update-user missing students.canEdit
- Severity: Medium · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken Function Level Authorization
- Location: src/app/api/admin/update-user/route.ts:24-45,145 — requiredRoles:['admin','moderator'], sensitive-field blocklist for moderators (SENSITIVE_FIELDS, lines 30-44), no students.canEdit.
- Impact: a moderator with students.canEdit=false can still edit any student's non-sensitive fields (name, contact details, photo, parent info) for an arbitrary uid. Escalation prevented by the blocklist (role, busId, routeId, shift, status, paymentAmount, validUntil, … are rejected with 403).
- Recommendation: call requireModeratorPermission(auth,'students','canEdit') before processing.
RBAC-07 — POST /api/admin/bus-fee lets a moderator change the global bus fee
- Severity: Medium-High · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken Function Level Authorization / privilege inconsistency
- Location: src/app/api/admin/bus-fee/route.ts:33-45,72 (requiredRoles:['admin','moderator']; the only resolveUserRole use is to build a notification sender name) → src/lib/bus-fee-service.ts:100-142 (no role check anywhere in the write path). The equivalent setting route settings/bus-fees correctly requires admin (settings/bus-fees/route.ts:92), and admin/update-user explicitly forbids moderators from touching financial fields (admin/update-user/route.ts:30-44).
- Impact: any moderator, including one with zero permissions or suspended status, rewrites the system-wide fee that drives payment flows, and an automatic broadcast notification is issued attributed to userRole:'admin' (route.ts:52).
- Compensating controls: Zod BusFeeUpdateSchema; history kept (bus-fee-service.ts:121-129); not a student-level escalation.
- Recommendation: requiredRoles:['admin'] (or require payments permission + admin) and drop the misleading 'admin' sender label.
RBAC-08 — admin/firestore-cleanup destructive maintenance available to moderators
- Severity: Medium · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken Function Level Authorization
- Location: src/app/api/admin/firestore-cleanup/route.ts:14,84,91 (POST and GET, requiredRoles:['admin','moderator'], no permission check)
- Impact: a moderator can irreversibly DELETE rows older than a caller-chosen daysOld from active_trips, reassignment_logs (audit trail), waiting_flags (route.ts:30-73) and batch-delete Firestore trip_sessions. Audit-log destruction and operational data loss by a non-admin/zero-permission account.
- Recommendation: requiredRoles:['admin']; if moderators must run it, require an explicit admin permission and clamp daysOld.
RBAC-09 — admin/sync-bus-counts bulk mutation without admin-only gate
- Severity: Low-Medium · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken Function Level Authorization
- Location: src/app/api/admin/sync-bus-counts/route.ts:8 (requiredRoles:['admin','moderator'], recomputes and writes currentMembers/shift counts/stopCounts for every bus).
- Impact: mass overwrite of fleet occupancy state by a non-admin/zero-permission moderator (integrity of seat availability).
- Recommendation: admin-only, or require buses.canEdit.
RBAC-10 (design gap, 16 routes) — Role-only surfaces have no permission category
- Severity: Low (design/defence-in-depth) · Confidence: High · Classification: Incomplete authorisation model
- Routes: admin/dashboard-counts, admin/deadline-config, analytics, fleet/live-status, metrics/json, notifications/create, payment/analytics, payment/export, payment/transactions, receipt/verify, reassignment-logs, reassignment-logs/write, sre/incidents, sre/slo, tracing/diagnostics/[traceId], tracing/search, tracing/servicemap.
- Description: all correctly require admin|moderator, but ModeratorPermissions (students|drivers|buses|routes|applications|payments) has no category for dashboards, analytics, payment reads, audit-log writes or observability. A moderator therefore always has: bulk payment export incl. decrypted student IDs/names/transaction IDs (payment/export/route.ts:40-60), arbitrary audit-log insertion (reassignment-logs/write/route.ts:30-44), unrestricted mass-notification broadcast to all students/drivers (notifications/create/route.ts:47-77,290+), and full infra telemetry.
- Recommendation: either declare these "moderator by role" explicitly in the security model, or add payments.canExport/observability.canView style flags.
RBAC-11 — Suspending a moderator does not remove moderator role
- Severity: Medium · Confidence: High · Classification: Authorisation lifecycle / privilege revocation
- Location: src/app/api/moderators/[id]/status/route.ts (updates moderator_profiles.status, invalidates caches) → identity.repository.pg.ts:702 (pgRemoveModerator touches only moderator_profiles) → role-cache.ts:89-90 + file header ("reads from PostgreSQL users table only").
- Description: suspension flips moderator_profiles.status, and getModeratorPermissions correctly returns ZERO_MODERATOR_PERMISSIONS for non-active status, so permission-gated routes deny. But users.role stays 'moderator', so every ROLE and RBAC route above still admits the suspended moderator: student/driver/application lists, dashboards, analytics, payment export, notifications broadcast, admin/update-user, admin/bus-fee, admin/firestore-cleanup.
- Impact: admins' mental model of "suspended = access removed" is only ~half true.
- Recommendation: on suspend/inactive, either null/downgrade users.role, or make verifyApiAuth/withSecurity reject role==='moderator' when moderator_profiles.status !== 'active'.
- (Deletion is handled correctly: moderators/[id]/delete → deleteUserAndData → row removal + invalidateCachedRole)
AUTH-01 — Proxy documents API authentication/RBAC it does not perform
- Severity: Low (defence-in-depth) · Confidence: High · Classification: Security control misrepresentation
- Location: src/proxy.ts:4-11 (claims auth + RBAC) vs :380-391 (only sets X-Proxy-Auth: none, never blocks).
- Impact: any future route that forgets a guard is silently public (this is exactly how the 10 PUB routes and routes/[id]/stops exist). Mitigated today by the per-route audit performed here.
- Recommendation: either implement a default-deny allowlist for /api/*, or correct the header comment.
AUTH-02 — Firebase ID tokens accepted in the request body on 34 routes
- Severity: Low · Confidence: High · Classification: Token handling / exposure
- Location: api-security.ts:73-74,96-106,304-310,397-398 (allowBodyToken, query idToken correctly never accepted, idToken stripped before validation); enabled on 34 routes (e.g. admin/update-user:148, admin/create-user:417, all driver/* with allowBodyToken, payment/razorpay/*, receipt/verify, location/update, student/* …).
- Impact: body-borne tokens are far more likely to land in proxy/WAF/APM logs and crash reports than header tokens. No log currently prints the token (verified api-security.ts logging and JSON.stringify(body) call sites).
- Recommendation: retire allowBodyToken for web callers; keep header-only.
AUTH-03 — GET /api/check-first-user unauthenticated bootstrap disclosure
- Severity: Low · Confidence: High · Classification: Information disclosure
- Location: src/app/api/check-first-user/route.ts (no guard; listed public in proxy.ts:88); returns isFirstUser = (users count === 0).
- Impact: unauthenticated caller learns whether any account exists; useful only in combination with create-first-admin, which itself requires FIRST_ADMIN_BOOTSTRAP_SECRET (absent from .env → fail-closed) and an empty users table (create-first-admin/route.ts:63-96).
- Recommendation: return a constant false after first bootstrap, or gate behind the secret.
AUTH-04 — GET /api/metrics unauthenticated
- Severity: Low · Confidence: High · Classification: Information disclosure
- Location: src/app/api/metrics/route.ts (no guard, not in PUBLIC_API_ROUTES) — Prometheus text export; metrics/json is correctly admin|moderator (metrics/json/route.ts:16).
- Impact: process/runtime counters (and any labelled metrics) readable by anyone; reconnaissance value only.
- Recommendation: restrict to internal/cron secret or IP allowlist, consistent with the JSON variant.
AUTH-05 — GET /api/download any-authenticated fetch proxy
- Severity: Low · Confidence: High · Classification: Insufficient authorisation on an SSRF-shaped endpoint
- Location: src/app/api/download/route.ts:32 (withSecurity, roles:[]).
- Mitigation present: strict host allowlist (Cloudinary / Firebase Storage / Supabase public) and no arbitrary URL parameter.
- Impact: any logged-in account (including an unauth_users applicant) can pull arbitrary objects from the allowlisted buckets whose IDs they know; no ownership check.
- Recommendation: add ownership/path-prefix checks or scope to the caller's own assets.
AUTH-06 — GET /api/routes/[id]/stops authenticates but applies no role
- Severity: Info/Low · Confidence: High · Classification: Missing role check
- Location: src/app/api/routes/[id]/stops/route.ts:10-16 (await adminAuth.verifyIdToken(token) then no role test).
- Impact: none beyond what GET /api/routes/[id]/view already exposes unauthenticated; noted for consistency.
AUTH-07 — canAppearInModeratorList read from the wrong object level (control never applies)
- Severity: Low · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken access-control logic (visibility/privacy control)
- Location: src/app/api/moderators-list/route.ts:17 and src/app/api/moderators/get-all/route.ts:17 → permissions.canAppearInModeratorList !== false, but the stored/validated shape is nested: permissions.applications.canAppearInModeratorList (validation-schemas.ts:476, moderators/add/route.ts:29, types/moderator-permissions.ts:63, useModeratorPermissions.ts:231).
- Impact: the expression always evaluates true, so a moderator explicitly hidden from the student application form's moderator dropdown is still listed to any authenticated user (moderators-list uses verifyTokenOnly, no role) — defeats an operator-set privacy/visibility flag. Same bug in the admin list filter.
- Recommendation: read permissions?.applications?.canAppearInModeratorList (and decide whether a missing permissions object should default to visible).
AUTH-08 — POST /api/delete-image ownership predicate can never be satisfied for students
- Severity: Low (reliability; fail-closed, not exploitable) · Confidence: High · Classification: Broken ownership logic
- Location: src/app/api/delete-image/route.ts (publicId.includes(user.uid)), given Cloudinary public_id = <folder>/<random> from upload.
- Impact: students can never delete their own uploads (asset deletion only works for admin/moderator) — a broken control, not an auth bypass.
- Recommendation: persist an owner_uid → public_id mapping and match on it.
AUTH-09 — DELETE /api/cloudinary/delete is dead (always 501)
- Severity: Info · Confidence: High · Classification: Dead/unreachable control
- Location: src/app/api/cloudinary/delete/route.ts:5-7 — auth first, then unconditional 501 Not implemented.
- Impact: none; removed attack surface. Should be deleted or implemented (see AUTH-08).
AUTH-10 — POST /api/admin/alerts unauthenticated when AM_WEBHOOK_SECRET unset
- Severity: Low (non-production only) · Confidence: High · Classification: Missing authentication outside production
- Location: src/app/api/admin/alerts/route.ts:43-60 — fails closed with 500 in NODE_ENV==='production' if the secret is unset (verified: AM_WEBHOOK_SECRET is not in .env); when set, comparison is length-checked + timingSafeEqual.
- Impact: in dev/staging, anyone can inject in-app notifications broadcast to all admins (spam/phishing channel); in production the route is either secret-protected or hard-disabled.
AUTH-11 — Revocation latency in role and permission caches
- Severity: Low · Confidence: High · Classification: Authorisation lifecycle
- Location: role-cache.ts:23 (5-min TTL, REDIS_URL-dependent cross-node invalidation, header lines 11-13) · moderator-permissions.ts (60 s in-process LRU, no cross-node invalidation).
- Impact: after a role downgrade, up to 5 min of retained privilege per instance (worst case: indefinite on other instances if REDIS_URL unset); permission changes up to 60 s per instance. Invalidation hooks exist on the mutating routes (moderators/[id]/permissions|status, admin/create-user|update-user, students|drivers/[id]/status, cleanup-helpers.ts:128,130).
- Recommendation: confirm REDIS_URL in production; shorten TTL; publish permission invalidations on the same bus.
AUTH-12 — Role-less routes accept any cryptographically valid token, even for users deleted from users
- Severity: Low · Confidence: High · Classification: Missing user-existence check
- Location: api-auth.ts:124-135 and api-security.ts:362-369 deny only when requiredRoles.length > 0; verifyTokenOnly (api-auth.ts:162-177) does no role lookup at all. Routes with roles:[]: applications/submit|save-draft|check|my-status|my-application, payment/recover, unauth-users/create, download, upload, save-fcm-token, auth/user, get-rules-content, admin/deadline-config.
- Impact: a Firebase account already deleted from users/student_profiles keeps access to those routes for the ID-token lifetime; combined with IDOR-01 this widens the submit path (ownership is the only remaining control there).
- Recommendation: require resolved.role to be non-empty on all non-public routes.
AUTH-13 — Browser-shipped Supabase anon key vs. RLS/TO authenticated policies are inconsistent
- Severity: Low-Medium (potential) · Confidence: Medium — partially unverified, see §4 · Classification: Data-plane authorisation boundary
- Location: src/lib/supabase-client.ts:15-18 (anon key in the bundle), used from the browser by moderator/students/page.tsx:220, admin/buses, moderator/buses, DynamicStudentMap.tsx:243, useWaitingFlags.ts:40; RLS policies in COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:2491-2643 (almost all TO authenticated, with auth.uid()/user_has_role() predicates), anon-readable rows only active_trips where status='active' (:2550-2552) and all bus_locations (:2558-2560).
- Description: under the documented schema, direct browser queries to student_profiles/buses/waiting_flags with the anon key return zero rows (fail-closed) — yet the UI is built to read them, which means either those features are broken or the deployed policies differ from this file. Two concrete exposures if the file is what's deployed: unauthenticated live GPS of every bus and active-trip driver IDs readable by anyone with the public anon key.
- Recommendation: verify live policies/grants (§4 test plan); if public tracking is intended, scope the anon read to non-sensitive columns and document it; otherwise drop the anon policies.
AUTH-14 — e2e-signin staging sign-in surface
- Severity: Info (closed by default) · Confidence: High · Classification: Environment-gated surface
- Location: src/app/e2e-signin/page.tsx:39-41 (notFound() unless NEXT_PUBLIC_E2E_STAGING==='true', which is not present in .env); it only accepts a custom token minted by an operator script (scripts/staging/lib.ts:51 — the sole createCustomToken call site; no API route mints tokens) and still resolves the user's role from PostgreSQL. No admin bypass exists.
- Recommendation: ensure the staging Docker image build arg is never set for production images.
AUTH-15 — setup-admin-document returns stored admin record before the role check
- Severity: Info · Confidence: High · Classification: Check-order defect
- Location: src/app/api/setup-admin-document/route.ts:46-56 (getAdminById early-return with data: existingPgAdmin) precedes the userData.role !== 'admin' 403 at :70.
- Impact: only exploitable if a non-admin row already exists in admin_profiles; write path is admin-gated (:70-74). Negligible today; reorder the checks.

1. PostgREST/RPC exposure (highest value). NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY ships to browsers (supabase-client.ts), and transition_application_state is SECURITY DEFINER. ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES … REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:27) plus an explicit REVOKE ALL … FROM anon, authenticated list (:2704-2706, 29 functions) do not include transition_application_state, release_application_lock, save_draft-adjacent helpers, approve_*… some are listed, some are not.

Repeat for approve_application, delete_student_cascade_v1, execute_reassignment_rollback.

Important Details
- Authority (confirmed): deadline calendar = Firestore settings/deadline (calendar.repository.ts:12-13,47); bus fee = Firestore settings/config (config.service.ts:112); markers = Firestore system_markers (config.service.ts:315-333); applications/students/payments = PostgreSQL/Supabase.
- Persisted vs derived: saveConfig writes ONLY academicSessionStart{month,day}, urgentWarningThreshold.days, softBlock.warningText, hardDelete.criticalWarningText, contactInfo (calendar.repository.ts:187-208). Everything else (academicYear, renewalNotification, renewalDeadline, softBlock, hardDelete, timeline) is re-derived on EVERY read using deriveAcademicLifecycle(startMonth, startDay, new Date().getFullYear()) (:56-59, local time). Month/day are stable across years except leap-year Feb-29 anchors.
- deriveAcademicLifecycle semantics (deadline-computation.ts:148-216): effectiveYear = session END year. expiry = June 30, softBlock = July 1, hardDelete = July 1 of effectiveYear+2. i.e. hard delete is ~731 days after expiry, while stored metadata claims daysAfterDeadline: 60 / daysAfterSoftBlock: 45 (calendar.repository.ts:120-121) and renewalNotification.daysBeforeDeadline: 90 (:88, actual reminder1 = expiry−3 months = ~121 days). Those metadata fields are never read for computation (grep: only type defs + repository) — cosmetic but contradictory.
- There is NO single "current session" authority. ≥10 independent derivations, mixing UTC/local and full-date/month-only/calendar-year-only:
 1. getCurrentSessionStartYear (session-activation.service.ts:73) — UTC, full date vs academicSessionStart
 2. deriveCreationCategorisation (application-eligibility.ts:229) — local, month-only vs academicYear.anchorMonth (= expiry month = 5/June, not 6/July) → flips a month early in June; DEAD CODE
 3. resolveApplicationType/isUpcomingApplication (application-eligibility.ts:72,159) — local getFullYear(), no anchor
 4. submitFinal (application.service.ts:177) — local getFullYear(), no anchor
 5. getCurrentAcademicSessionStartYear() duplicated in admin/applications/[id]/page.tsx:293 and moderator/applications/[id]/page.tsx:319 — local, full date → disagrees with server for 5.30h on Jul 1 (IST)
 6. findActiveConfig (calendar.repository.ts:58) — local getFullYear() as effectiveYear
 7. expiry-check.ts:25 — UTC getUTCFullYear()
 8. SystemLifecycleIntelligence.tsx:68 — client local getFullYear()
 9. analytics.service.ts:120 / dashboard-counts/route.ts:125 — local getFullYear() as displayed year (negative "days left" Jul–Dec)
10. payment.service.ts:252,315, renewal-utils.ts:33, application.service.ts:380,747 — calendar-year base for validity
- Renewal/registration Jan-1 discontinuity: baseYear = new Date().getUTCFullYear() when validUntil is past (payment.service.ts:315-327, renewal-utils.ts:33-55, application.service.ts:380-388). Same fee: renewal on Dec 15 2026 → validUntil Jun 30 2027; on Jan 2 2027 → Jun 30 2028 (+12 months). Should be getCurrentSessionStartYear()+1.
- Phase 2 wiring is broken at its single write point. Client sends only {formData, needsCapacityReview} (apply/form/page.tsx:1329-1340); formData nests years at formData.sessionInfo.* (:97-102), but submitFinal reads formData.sessionStartYear || ... || body.startYear || 0 (application.service.ts:178-181). Result: sessionStartYear: 0, sessionEndYear: undefined, targetSession: undefined, eligibleApproval: undefined (:224-236). deriveCreationCategorisation, resolveEligibleApprovalAtCreation, computeFutureEligibleApproval have zero callers; no .tsx ever sends eligibleApproval/targetSession.
- Downstream of that: session-activation.service.ts:151,181 reads Number(app.targetSession?.startYear) → NaN → cron session filter short-circuits (:154) so ALL verified_upcoming activate regardless of target session, and :180-184 remaining-check never matches → activation_{year} marker written after one clean run. Cleanup cron "upcoming pass" filters application_type='future' AND state='submitted' (cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:326-330) — never matches → no reminders, no expired sweep. integrity-detector.ts:153 groups by targetSession → all collapse to ::none. UI always shows "Awaiting new academic session" (admin/applications/page.tsx:1387).
- Eligibility gate is not enforced server-side: application-eligibility.ts:126 claims "enforced server-side in both approval routes via isApprovalEligible"; grep shows isApprovalEligible has no server caller (getUpcomingStatus is used only in moderator/applications/page.tsx). Neither applications/approve/route.ts nor approve-unauth/route.ts calls it.
- Money path: CreateOrderSchema (validation-schemas.ts:167-175): notes is z.record(z.string(), …) (string-only → object injection blocked), but trustedDurationYears = durationYears || Number(notes?.duration || 1) (create-order/route.ts:20) uses unvalidated notes.duration. Amount is validated as busFee × durationYears (:26-34) — but validity at approval comes from the independent formData.sessionInfo.sessionEndYear − sessionStartYear (application.service.ts:747-749). No cross-check → 1-year fee can grant 4-year validity. Offline amountPaid is never fee-validated (client-declared; human approval is the control). Webhook (payment/webhook/razorpay/route.ts:77) re-binds payment↔order amount only, never re-reads busFee.
- Hardcoded fallback in money path: getSystemConfig() returns {busFee:{amount:5000}} when the doc is missing and caches it 5 min (config.service.ts:113-121), contradicting bus-fee-service.ts:63-64 "NO FALLBACK".
- Dual 5-min caches: deadline CACHE_TTL_MS (calendar.repository.ts:31-36) and config CONFIG_CACHE_TTL_MS (config.service.ts:89) are module-local, invalidated only in the writing process → multi-instance/edge staleness up to 5 min. Validation covers only academicSessionStart month/day (:146-171); syncSystemConfigDates is an intentional no-op (:138-141). No session-overlap/inverted-range validation anywhere; only session-activation.service.ts:228 checks endYear > startYear.

Client verify: src\app\api\payment\razorpay\verify-payment\route.ts:61-198 — signature before side effects (:66-79), trusted order notes (:100-106), owner binding trustedUserId === auth.uid (:108-114), payment↔order amount match (:116-126), status==='captured' (:128-134), then processCapturedPayment (:140-147), response pendingApproval only for renewal (:165-171).
6. Webhook: src\app\api\payment\webhook\razorpay\route.ts:15-110 — timingSafeEqual on raw body (:33-46), order fetched for trusted notes with 502-retry on failure (:63-69), order↔payment amount binding (:77-80), processCapturedPayment (:83-90), 500 on internal error → Razorpay retry (:93-99). Handles only payment.captured (:55).
7. Processing core: src\lib\payment\payment.service.ts:120-452 — idempotency read (:133), self-heal (:137-212), missing-id guard (:220-223), funnel split notes.type||notes.purpose (:228-229), new-registration ledger INSERT (:259-285), renewal ledger INSERT (:351-368), renewal application submitFinal (:378-404), staff notification (:407-435), sentinel mapping (:441-451).
8. Ledger write: src\lib\services\payments-supabase.ts:270-419 — pre-check SELECT on (student_uid, session_start_year, session_end_year, status='Completed') (:277-299), INSERT (:363-367), 23505 → ALREADY_PROCESSED/DUPLICATE_SESSION_PAYMENT (:369-406).
9. Entitlement (after staff approval, not at capture): src\domains\application\services\application.service.ts:462-479 (identity_activate_student_with_capacity), :511-525 (finalize_application_approval), side effects :530 → postCommitApprovalSideEffects:801-898.
B. Renewal — ONLINE
1. UI: src\app\student\renew\page.tsx:813 (purpose="renewal"), auto-recovery :155-188.
2. Same order/verify/webhook path as A (order type='renewal' from server).
3. processCapturedPayment renewal branch: resolve student (:295-305), validity math (:307-328), ledger INSERT (:351-368), submitFinal(..., {applicationId: online_<paymentId>}) (:378-404) → creates state='submitted' renewal application + staff notification (:407-435). Entitlement only after staff approves that application.
C. Renewal — OFFLINE
src\app\api\student\renew-service-v2\route.ts:64-140 — no payment row at submission (:108-111 explicit comment), application only, deterministic renewal_<uid>_<date> id (:67-68), 409 dedupe (:72-77). Online branch of same route is a second order-creation path with server-computed fee (:37-53).
D. Offline approval (payment row path)
src\app\api\payments\approve\route.ts → approveOfflinePayment payment.service.ts:583-751: CAS WHERE status='Pending' (:619-633, impl payments-supabase.ts:479-495), then student validity with 3 retries (:652-666), failure → audit PAYMENT_VALIDITY_SYNC_FAILED (:667-694), receipt signature best-effort (:698-703), audit payment_approved (:705-723). Reject: :760-830 (CAS; Completed protected at :771-773).
E. Approval-time ledger write (application approval)
application.service.ts:801-898: amount = app.amount_paid || app.form_data?.paymentInfo?.amountPaid || 0 (:812) — applications has no amount_paid/payment_mode columns (supabase\COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:216-241, only payment_id at :240) → only client JSON is read; online → upsertApprovalPayment (:818-840) → upsertPayment (overwrite, payments-supabase.ts:170-263); offline → createOfflinePaymentAtApproval (:841-866 → payment.service.ts:468-552); all failures .catch(console.error) (:839,:864), Promise.allSettled (:897). verifiedBy='system_online_payment' is client-declared (application.service.ts:229-231); submit-final has no Zod schema (src\app\api\applications\submit-final\route.ts:42).
F. Recovery / read surfaces
Recover: src\app\api\payment\recover\route.ts — ownership (:84,:149,:153), Redis SETNX throttle (:30-41), payment-entity notes (:268) → processCapturedPayment (:270-276), statuses (:186-196,:286,:295,:305,:328). Reads: receipt src\app\api\payment\receipt\[paymentId]\route.ts:83-150 (own/moderator-permission), src\app\api\receipt\verify\route.ts:62-226 (admin/moderator + payments permission :51-60), transactions src\app\api\payment\transactions\route.ts:156-168 (student → own uid) / admin branch, export src\app\api\payment\export\route.ts (requiredRoles:['admin','moderator'], no permission check), analytics (admin/moderator), src\app\api\student\payment-history\route.ts:20-28 (self-only), src\app\api\payments\[paymentId]\route.ts:63-70 (owner/admin/moderator, manual auth, no withSecurity). Manual bootstrap: src\app\api\admin\create-user\route.ts:198-213 (payment row before entitlement).
2. IDEMPOTENCY ANALYSIS
Real exactly-once primitive: payments.payment_id TEXT UNIQUE (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:283) + read-then-insert (payment.service.ts:133, payments-supabase.ts:277-299,363-406). Two concurrent writers with the same payment id: loser gets 23505 → fallback lookup → ALREADY_PROCESSED → already_processed, and correctly does not run side effects. This path is sound.
Where it breaks:
- Different payment ids, same student+session: only a SELECT pre-check exists (payments-supabase.ts:277-299); there is no unique index on (student_uid, session_start_year, session_end_year) nor on razorpay_payment_id (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:274-311). TOCTOU → two Completed rows for one entitlement (double-count), or the inverse: a genuinely second capture is rejected as DUPLICATE_SESSION_PAYMENT and never written to the ledger (payment.service.ts:446-449) with no refund/reconciliation.
- Side-effect idempotency relies on applicationId = online_<paymentId> PK (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:217) + partial unique idx_applications_active_student_session (:268-270). If an application already exists, submitFinal returns success:false and callers ignore it (payment.service.ts:379-404).
- Approval ledger write is an UPSERT, not an insert (payments-supabase.ts:247-251, onConflict:'payment_id') → PK-violation protection is replaced by silent overwrite of amount / student_uid / transaction_date / valid_until / approved_by / document_signature.
- Validity is a non-transactional read-modify-write with a monotonic max guard (src\domains\student\services\student.service.ts:60-71) — safe against regression of validUntil, but sessionStartYear is not monotonic (:91) and status:'active' is forced unconditionally (:93).
- Dead idempotency infrastructure: processed_payments + processed_payments_acquire/release/cleanup RPCs (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:326,1051-1080) have zero app callers; deleteExpiredProcessedPayments() is a no-op stub (src\lib\notification-expiry.ts:19-27).
- Recover throttle is best-effort: any Redis error → throttle skipped (recover\route.ts:34-41).
- Post-commit side effects are non-atomic and failures swallowed (application.service.ts:839,:864,:897).
3. FINDINGS
PAY-01 — CONFIRMED PRODUCTION BUG (CRITICAL) — Every renewal path fails: submitFinal requires shift, no renewal caller supplies it
- Evidence: submitFinal throws unconditionally when shift is absent — C:\Users\ADMIN\Desktop\Projects\ITMS\src\domains\application\services\application.service.ts:192-200 (rawShift read only from formData.shift|selectedShift|body.shift; normalizeShift(undefined) → null, src\lib\utils\shift-utils.ts:51-52). All three renewal callers pass no shift: online renewal src\lib\payment\payment.service.ts:382-404; self-heal :182-204; offline renewal src\app\api\student\renew-service-v2\route.ts:80-99. Order notes contain no shift (src\components\PaymentModeSelector.tsx:405-413; create-order\route.ts:56-67).
- Impact: Online: ledger row inserted first (payment.service.ts:351-368), then submitFinal throws → outer catch rethrows (:441-450) → verify-payment returns 500 (verify-payment\route.ts:149-155) and webhook returns 500 forever (webhook\route.ts:93-99,:106-109); every retry hits isPaymentProcessed → true → self-heal → throws again → status:'error'. Result: money captured and recorded as Completed, no renewal application, staff notification skipped (:407-435), student sees an error, Razorpay retries exhaust. Offline: always HTTP 503 (renew-service-v2\route.ts:100-106).
- Reachable from: src\app\student\renew\page.tsx:813 (purpose="renewal") and :346 (offline).
- Compensating controls: funds are not lost (ledger row exists, payments history shows it); manual staff renewal remains possible.
PAY-02 — CONFIRMED PRODUCTION BUG (HIGH) — submitFinal result ignored → payment captured with no renewal record ("success" without entitlement request)
- Evidence: application.service.ts:170-175 returns {success:false, error:'An application is already in progress'} (no throw) when a live application exists; callers at payment.service.ts:382-404 and :182-204 never inspect the return value and fall through to return {status:'success'} (:439,:205); renew-service-v2\route.ts:80-99 same.
- Impact: Captured renewal payment reported as success/awaiting approval while no application referencing that paymentId exists → the paid amount is never linked to a reviewable renewal; staff are not notified.
PAY-03 — FINANCIAL INTEGRITY DEFECT (HIGH) — Approval-time ledger write takes amount from client-controlled form JSON
- Evidence: application.service.ts:812 (app.amount_paid || app.form_data?.paymentInfo?.amountPaid); applications has no such columns (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:216-241); paymentInfo is written by the browser (Step4ServicePayment.tsx:189-197,:203); submit-final applies no schema (src\app\api\applications\submit-final\route.ts:42); amount then written/overwritten into the ledger (application.service.ts:818-840 → payments-supabase.ts:217-251). No server-side re-verification against Razorpay at approval time (approve path application.service.ts:301-533 contains no payment check).
- Impact: The immutable ledger's amount, transaction_date (reset to approval time), valid_until, approved_by and document_signature are recomputed from client-declared values. Staff UI displays the same client value (src\app\admin\applications\[id]\page.tsx:1246), so the discrepancy is invisible.
- Compensating controls: staff approval is required; online capture path independently writes the authoritative amount (payment.service.ts:259-285) — but it is later overwritten.
PAY-04 — FINANCIAL INTEGRITY DEFECT / SECURITY VULNERABILITY (HIGH) — Cross-student ledger overwrite via client-supplied razorpayPaymentId
- Evidence: application.service.ts:822 uses app.form_data?.paymentInfo?.razorpayPaymentId || app.payment_id || \pay_${Date.now()}\` as the upsert key; upsertPayment performs upsert(..., {onConflict:'payment_id'}) replacing student_uid, student_name, amount, valid_until (payments-supabase.ts:217-251). student_uid has no unique/RK guard (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:276`).
- Impact: A crafted formData.paymentInfo.razorpayPaymentId pointing at another student's payment rewrites that row's owner and amount at approval; a missing id creates a phantom Completed row (pay_<timestamp>) with a client amount → revenue over/under-statement and no razorpay_payment_id for reconciliation.
- Compensating controls: approval requires staff action; rows are not deleted.
PAY-05 — FINANCIAL INTEGRITY DEFECT (HIGH) — Duplicate-payment handling is check-then-act with no DB uniqueness, and the opposite failure silently drops captured money
- Evidence: pre-check SELECT then INSERT, no unique index on (student_uid, session_start_year, session_end_year) or razorpay_payment_id (payments-supabase.ts:277-299,363-367; COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:274-311); DUPLICATE_SESSION_PAYMENT → already_paid_for_session (payment.service.ts:446-449) → surfaced as success by verify-payment\route.ts:157-190 (only status==='error' fails).
- Impact: (a) two concurrent distinct captures for the same session both insert → double revenue for one entitlement; (b) a second real capture (two orders, one student/session) is discarded from the ledger — money collected at Razorpay, no row, no refund, no reconciliation. upsertApprovalPayment's overwrite behaviour (PAY-03/04) is the third inconsistent writer.
PAY-06 — FINANCIAL INTEGRITY DEFECT (HIGH) — Offline/renewal money never enters the ledger
- Evidence: offline renewal creates no payment row at submission (renew-service-v2\route.ts:108-111); at approval the write is gated on amount > 0 (application.service.ts:812-813), but renewal formData has no paymentInfo.amountPaid (built at payment.service.ts:385-399 and renew-service-v2\route.ts:83-94) → both branches skipped → no ledger row is ever written for an approved offline renewal (and, per PAY-01, for online renewals the application never exists).
- Impact: Understated revenue, no audit trail for the most common cash flow, receipts unavailable (receipt route requires a Completed row, receipt\[paymentId]\route.ts:105-115).
PAY-07 — SPECIFICATION/BUSINESS-POLICY DECISION + FINANCIAL INTEGRITY DEFECT (MEDIUM) — Entitlement can be granted without a verified payment
- Evidence: approve() performs capacity/identity/finalize RPCs and only then best-effort payment side effects (application.service.ts:407-525); there is no gate requiring a Completed ledger row or a Razorpay capture check; verifiedBy:'system_online_payment' is derived from client formData.paymentInfo.paymentMode (:229-231).
- Compensating controls: staff review of payment evidence in the moderation UI; the money-before-entitlement direction used by create-user (create-user\route.ts:196-213) and by online capture (ledger before approval) is correct elsewhere.
PAY-08 — RELIABILITY DEFECT (MEDIUM) — The reconciliation the code comments promise does not exist
- Evidence: PAYMENT_VALIDITY_SYNC_FAILED is written-only (payment.service.ts:674); WEBHOOK_PAYMENT_SYNC_PENDING is written only from saveTransaction (payment-transaction.service.ts:359), which has no callers (:128 definition only). Cron set = cleanup-expired-students|cleanup-notifications|cleanup-stale-locks|cleanup-trip-history|expiry-check|integrity-sweep|session-activation — none reads payments; integrity-scan|integrity-sweep run only bus/seat/app checks (src\lib\services\integrity-detector.ts). No code compares ledger vs Razorpay vs entitlement.
- Impact: approveOfflinePayment failing after 3 attempts leaves Completed payment + no entitlement with only a log line (payment.service.ts:667-694) — permanent divergence, never repaired. Marketing copy claims the opposite (src\app\(marketing)\how-it-works\page.tsx:129).
PAY-09 — DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT (MEDIUM) — Post-commit financial side effects are fire-and-forget
- Evidence: application.service.ts:819-866 (.catch(err => console.error(...))), :897 (Promise.allSettled), called after the finalize RPC (:530).
- Impact: Approval succeeds while the ledger insert fails → revenue under-recording with no retry, queue, or marker. Combined with PAY-05/06 this is the primary "money recorded but missing" generator.
PAY-10 — DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT (MEDIUM) — Idempotency infrastructure is dead while the code claims otherwise
- Evidence: processed_payments + acquire/release/cleanup RPCs (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:326,1051-1080) have zero application references (only the schema file matches); deleteExpiredProcessedPayments() returns {deletedPayments:0} unconditionally (src\lib\notification-expiry.ts:19-27); header comment claims "Atomic idempotency check inside transaction" and "Processed payment marker set BEFORE student update" (webhook\route.ts:4-7) — neither is implemented (no transaction; marker is the ledger row itself).
- Compensating control: payment_id UNIQUE genuinely covers the same-id race (§2).
PAY-11 — DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT (MEDIUM) — Schema/code mismatch on payments + silently dropped columns
- Evidence: PAYMENT_COLUMNS selects purpose (payments-supabase.ts:85) but payments in the only schema file has no purpose column (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:274-311); no ADD COLUMN ... purpose exists anywhere (repo-wide grep, only .sql file = COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql). createPayment/upsertPayment never write metadata, purpose, application_id, renewal_id (payments-supabase.ts:217-251,:338-357) although CreatePaymentInput declares metadata (:51-52).
- Impact: If the live DB matches the repo schema, every read (getPaymentById, history, receipts, approvals) fails and is swallowed to null/[] (:715,:740,:767…). If the live DB has the column, the repo schema is incomplete (drift). Either way receipt/verify echoes purpose: payment.purpose and metadata: payment.metadata (src\app\api\receipt\verify\route.ts:195-200) and the PDF faculty (receipt\[paymentId]\route.ts:79) are permanently empty.
PAY-12 — SECURITY VULNERABILITY (MEDIUM) — Recovery trusts payment-entity notes, defeating the webhook's explicit hardening
- Evidence: webhook deliberately uses order notes and documents that payment notes "can differ from order notes" (webhook\route.ts:58-62,71-72); recover passes razorpayPayment.notes (recover\route.ts:268-276). In the already-processed branch the funnel is inferred from those notes with rawPurpose = notes.type||notes.purpose; empty rawPurpose evaluates to renewal (payment.service.ts:142-144) and the self-heal path has no !enrollmentId && !userId guard (that guard exists only on the first-pass path, :220-223), then calls submitFinal(studentUid='') (:156-204).
- Impact: An already-processed new-registration payment whose payment notes lack type causes recover (invoked automatically from src\app\student\page.tsx:100, src\app\apply\status\page.tsx:45, student\renew\page.tsx:155-188) to fabricate a spurious online_<paymentId> renewal application — an extra entitlement request and duplicate staff notification, potentially with an empty applicant_uid.
PAY-13 — FINANCIAL INTEGRITY DEFECT / SPECIFICATION DECISION (MEDIUM) — No refund capability; only payment.captured is handled
- Evidence: webhook accepts solely payment.captured (webhook\route.ts:55); no refund API exists (only UI strings payment-state.ts:174,266,279… and unused type fields payment-history.service.ts:39-42); refund grep across src\ returns no implementation.
- Impact: A dashboard refund/refusal leaves the row Completed with entitlement intact, and there is no event path to reverse it. Combined with PAY-05(b) (captured-but-unrecorded) there is no way to reconcile either direction.
PAY-14 — SECURITY VULNERABILITY (LOW–MEDIUM) — Access-control / origin-validation inconsistencies on payment surfaces
- Evidence: (a) payment/export allows any moderator to export the full ledger including decrypted PII (src\app\api\payment\export\route.ts requiredRoles:['admin','moderator'], no requireModeratorPermission), whereas receipt/verify and receipt download do require payments permission (receipt\verify\route.ts:51-60; receipt\[paymentId]\route.ts:50-56,130-132). (b) src\app\api\payments\[paymentId]\route.ts:15-29 hand-rolls auth — no withSecurity (no rate limit, no schema, no CSRF wrapper); ownership is enforced (:63-70, GET-only). (c) Two divergent origin implementations: api-security.ts:220 allows any *.vercel.app/*.adtu.in while proxy.ts:232-239 is strict; the test suite only asserts the strict one (src\lib\security\__tests__\security-boundaries-master.test.ts:17-20); custom OPTIONS handlers return permissive Access-Control-Allow-Origin for any *.vercel.app (verify-payment\route.ts:200-216, create-order\route.ts:110-134).
- Compensating controls: the Next 16 proxy (src\proxy.ts:355,:334-342 — validated as the active convention for next ^16.3.0) enforces strict origin + global IP rate limit before handlers, and webhook is exempt by design (proxy.ts:255-258). Auth is mandatory for all payment routes (api-security.ts:320-369; requiredRoles: [] = "any authenticated", :67). Rate limits: create 5/min, verify 10/min (rate-limiter.ts:174-175).
PAY-15 — CONSISTENCY DEFECT (LOW) — Status vocabulary divergence + dead duplicate payment modules
- Evidence: table has no status CHECK (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:281, values used Pending|Completed|Rejected); payment-state.ts:34 declares PaymentDatabaseStatus = 'Pending'|'Completed'|'Failed' (Failed is never written); legacy payment-history.service.ts:33 uses pending|success|failed|refunded (module has no importers). Dead code: createOnlinePayment (payment.service.ts:54, no callers), PaymentTransactionService.saveTransaction/upsertCompletedTransaction (payment-transaction.service.ts:128,:90, no callers, Firestore-fallback path included), paymentsSupabaseService.deletePayment intentionally blocked (payments-supabase.ts:508).
- Impact: future writers can introduce unmapped statuses; dead modules are re-use hazards (they perform upsertPayment overwrites).
PAY-16 — CONSISTENCY DEFECT (LOW) — Idempotent approval re-activates students and can regress session start year
- Evidence: approveOfflinePayment already-Completed branch re-applies validity (payment.service.ts:595-607); applyPaymentValidity unconditionally sets status:'active' and uses non-monotonic sessionStartYear fallback (src\domains\student\services\student.service.ts:87-94).
- Impact: a duplicate/replayed approve call reactivates a withdrawn/deactivated student; validUntil itself is protected by the max guard (:66-71).
Verified-OK controls (with compensating value): server-side amount authority in create-order:26-34 and renew-service-v2:37-42; webhook signature-before-side-effects with timingSafeEqual (webhook:33-46); verify-payment owner binding (:108-114) + amount binding (:116-126); payments RLS TO service_role inserts / TO PUBLIC USING(false) delete (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:2538-2546) with app traffic on the service-role client (src\lib\supabase\supabase-server.ts:18); no delete path for payments (payments-supabase.ts:508-510); offline approve/reject CAS on status='Pending' (:479-495) and Completed/Rejected guards (payment.service.ts:595-616,:771-783); reject of an application can only touch Pending rows (application.service.ts:593-603 → domains\payment\services\payment.service.ts:88-93); student-scoped reads (payment-history\route.ts:23-28, transactions:168, receipt:119-128, recover:84,:149,:153); receipt download gated on Completed + moderator permission.
4. UNVERIFIED
1. Razorpay platform behaviour: whether payment-entity notes inherit order notes (decides PAY-12 trigger frequency); webhook registration/RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET presence in prod (route 500s without it, webhook:27-31); retry/backoff schedule and terminal-failure handling; availability of order.paid/payment.failed/refund.* events; partial captures.
2. Live database state: whether payments.purpose (and metadata) actually exist — repo schema says no (PAY-11); any CHECK constraints/triggers beyond COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql; whether RLS policies are ENABLEd as written; whether ad-hoc columns were added outside the repo (only one .sql file exists).
3. Deployment: that production serves this build with src/proxy.ts active under the Next 16 proxy convention (the strict CSRF + IP rate-limit layer depends on it); real CORS/ALLOWED_ORIGINS/NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL values.
4. Runtime reproduction: PAY-01/PAY-02 are proven by static call-path analysis only (read-only mandate — no tests executed, no requests sent). Whether any operator-side UI supplies shift to renewal submission was not observable from the server code paths audited.
5. Approval UI checks: whether moderation screens perform an out-of-band Razorpay lookup before approving (client code not treated as authoritative for this audit).
6. Existing production data: whether historical applications.form_data.paymentInfo values match captured amounts (PAY-03 blast radius), and whether duplicate Completed rows already exist (PAY-05).
7. Redis semantics for the recover throttle (fail-open confirmed in code; production Redis availability/topology not verified).

Approval never double-applies	processing_lock lease (validate_application_for_approval L841-856, finalize_* L900-986) + seat RPC	Good for same application; not idempotent across retries (DB-04)
Payment processed exactly once	App-level SELECT check (payment.service.ts:1093-1111); processed_payments_acquire L1051 never called by code	TOCTOU (DB-07)
Single-admin invariant	None — users.role CHECK L82 only	Not enforced
Soft/valid-until expiry	soft_block_student_with_seat_release L1572, cron cleanup-expired-students	App/RPC-level
Referential integrity	No FKs — hand-written RPC cascades delete_student_cascade_v1 (L1912), delete_route_cascade_v1 (L1977)	Must be perfect in app code (DB-05)
2. RLS MATRIX
Legend: S service-role (server, supabase-server.ts), A authenticated JWT, N anon, — denied. Policies that reference auth.role() = 'service_role' inside a TO authenticated policy are dead code (Postgres sets role to authenticated for JWT clients) — marked †.

Verified compensating controls (strong):
- Browser is read-only. The anon/auth client (src\lib\supabase-client.ts) is imported by exactly 8 files; a scan for .insert(|.update(|.delete(|.upsert( in all 8 returned zero matches. All writes go through the service-role singleton (src\lib\supabase-server.ts).
- Route-level authorization independent of RLS: src\app\api\students\route.ts (verifyApiAuth(request, ['admin','moderator','driver']) + rate limit), src\app\api\applications\approve\route.ts (resolveUserRole/requireModeratorPermission), run-route (requireAdmin L21-34). Sufficient as a second layer given the coarse USING(true) reads.

*DB-13 | CAPACITY / INVARIANTS | P3 | CONFIDENCE: HIGH | MISSING CONSTRAINT | supabase\COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql | buses | direct service-role writes; delete_route_cascade_v1 | DDL + RPC | Load ≤ capacity and current_members = morning + evening* | buses has capacity > 0, morning_load >= 0, evening_load >= 0 (L176-178) but no CHECK (morning_load <= capacity), no CHECK (current_members >= 0), and no CHECK (current_members = morning_load + evening_load); delete_route_cascade_v1:1997-2003 nulls student_profiles.bus_id without touching counters; Seat.releaseSeat/bus_decrement_capacity are the only decrements | Counter drift is possible through any path that bypasses the RPCs (service role, future code, cascade deletes) | The three-way denormalization is the classic drift source | Add CHECK (current_members >= 0 AND current_members = morning_load + evening_load) and CHECK (morning_load <= capacity AND evening_load <= capacity); make delete_route_cascade_v1 decrement counters (or reconcile immediately) | Silently wrong capacity → overbooking or false "full" | Always | Data integrity | Compensating control: src\lib\services\admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts (recounts seat-owning students and rewrites all counters, with delta alerting at threshold 5) — adequate but manual/optional, not scheduled | Add the CHECKs + hook reconciliation into cron | COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:176-178,1997-2003,786-806; src\lib\services\admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:1-80
*DB-14 | SECURITY / HYGIENE | P4 | CONFIDENCE: MEDIUM | SECURITY POSTURE / MISCONFIGURATION | supabase\COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql, src\lib\supabase-server.ts, src\lib\supabase-client.ts | key handling, cleanup_* functions | runtime | file + repo grep | Secrets stay server-side; SECURITY DEFINER functions pin search_path* | Good: SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY appears only in src\lib\supabase-server.ts (server singleton) — no service key in any .tsx, page, or component (browser uses anon key only); Good: 40 of 45 SECURITY DEFINER functions set SET search_path = public; Gap: 5 do not — cleanup_old_trip_history (L1451), cleanup_stale_device_sessions (L1465), expire_waiting_flags (L1476), get_stale_locks (L1498), get_reassignment_logs (L1556); Gap: no GRANT/REVOKE on any table | Unpinned search_path on SECURITY DEFINER is the documented privilege-escalation pattern (object-shadowing if the role can create objects) | SET search_path = public on the remaining 5 (and on driver_location_updates-related code) | Low likelihood, high impact if reachable | Conditional on schema-create privileges for anon/authenticated | Defense in depth | None needed beyond the fix | COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1451,1465,1476,1498,1556; src\lib\supabase-server.ts; src\lib\supabase-client.ts
4. UNVERIFIED (explicitly out of reach in read-only, no-DB mode)
 1. Live schema vs file — drift direction. Whether the deployed DB contains payments.purpose, waiting_flags.student_profile_photo, routes.total_stops, buses.driver_uid, audit_events.metadata, notifications.content, active_trips.fcm_start_sent, moderator_profiles.permissions, fcm_tokens.valid/token_hash/last_seen, migration_log, payment_ledger, realtime_driver_locations. If they exist live, the file is stale (recovery gap); if not, DB-01 paths fail at runtime. Suggested read-only check: SELECT table_name, column_name FROM information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema='public' ORDER BY 1,2;
 2. Actual EXECUTE privileges for anon/authenticated on the 45 SECURITY DEFINER functions (DB-02/DB-03 depend on Supabase's ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES still granting EXECUTE). Check: SELECT p.proname, has_function_privilege('anon',p.oid,'EXECUTE'), has_function_privilege('authenticated',p.oid,'EXECUTE') FROM pg_proc p JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid=p.pronamespace WHERE n.nspname='public' AND p.prosecdef;
 3. service_role BYPASSRLS attribute. The file's 13 service_role_bypass_* policies (L2646-2659) imply uncertainty. If BYPASSRLS is absent, payments (no service SELECT policy, L2535-2546), waiting_flags, device_sessions and driver_trip_history writes/reads by the server client would be denied → widespread failure. Check: SELECT rolname, rolbypassrls FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname IN ('service_role','anon','authenticated');
 4. FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY — not present in the file; not confirmable for the live DB (affects owner-path behaviour only).
 5. Which tables are in supabase_realtime publication (file adds active_trips, bus_locations, L2719-2729) and whether realtime sends respect RLS for the subscribing role.
 6. Whether d1–d7 data migrations were run and against what Firestore state; whether migration_log exists live; idempotency of each up().
 7. Route-level authz completeness — spot-checked students, applications/approve, migrations/run-route, payment/webhook (HMAC + timing-safe + amount binding verified). The other ~12 routes with no obvious auth pattern (admin/alerts, buses/[id]/*, check-first-user, create-first-admin, faculties, get-bus-fee, landing-video, metrics, payments/[paymentId], routes/[id]/view) were opened only at the two I judged acceptable (webhook secret; post-fetch ownership check) — full coverage belongs to the API-authz agent.
 8. No dynamic/raw SQL — confirmed by grep (no SELECT … FROM/INSERT INTO/UPDATE … SET strings, no .execute(/.query(/.raw(, all access via PostgREST builder + 41 .rpc(...) call sites enumerated). Not verified: prepared-statement or db.rpc payload injection inside RPC parameters (plpgsql uses format() only in the two DO blocks, L2656-2657, 2704-2706 — static formats).
 9. processed_operations / temporary_assignments / processed_payments are in schema but never referenced by src (dead tables) — cannot confirm whether an external job uses them.
10. Application-layer flows (Agent E territory): seat release on expiry, renewal validity math, session activation batch — only the DB-side primitives were audited here.
Bottom line
The database layer's concurrency design is the strongest part (row locks under FOR UPDATE, CAS updates, application processing leases, atomic trip/RPC operations), and the browser-read-only + service-role-server split is a genuine compensating control that largely contains the coarse RLS. The blockers are configuration, not design: (a) the declared source-of-truth DDL no longer matches the code across ~10 tables and 3 missing tables (DB-01), (b) a policy helper function is revoked from the very roles whose policies call it (DB-02), (c) 14 dangerous SECURITY DEFINER functions are left outside the revoke list (DB-03), and (d) approval finalization is non-idempotent with a dead compensation hook (DB-04). DB-01 and DB-02 must be resolved before any production apply; DB-04/DB-05/DB-06 govern financial/capacity/compliance correctness; DB-07–DB-14 are follow-ups.
▣  Explore · MiMo-V2.6-Flash Free · 11m 56s








ADTU ITMS — AGENT P: API / SHARED SECURITY BOUNDARY

2. RATE LIMITER ANALYSIS
2.1 All limiter instances in the system
#	Limiter	Location	Scope / key	Limit	Window	Memory bounded?	Distributed?	Failure mode
L1	Proxy global IP	proxy.ts:25-63, 334-342	getClientIp (x-real-ip→cf→XFF rightmost)	300 req	60 s	YES — IP_CACHE_MAX 50 000; at cap scans ≤1500 then FIFO-evicts 500	NO (per process)	429 + Retry-After:60; bypass via x-load-test-bypass when STAGING_MODE+LOAD_TEST_SECRET (both absent from env → disabled)
L2	HTTP per-endpoint	rate-limiter.ts:17-59, 79-119	uid:pathname (createRateLimitId:235) or ip:<ip>:<path> when skipAuth	see §2.2	60 s (etc.)	PARTIAL — maxSize = 10 000, FIFO (not LRU) eviction at :36-39	NO — Upstash path dead (§2.3); fail-open to local memory on error (:158-160)	429 with X-RateLimit-* + Retry-After
