2.2 RateLimits.* — actual values (rate-limiter.ts:167-211)
AUTH 10/60s      LOGIN 5/60s          PASSWORD_RESET 3/300s     CRON 5/60s
PAYMENT_CREATE 5/60s   PAYMENT_VERIFY 10/60s

CREATE 30/60s    READ 100/60s         UPDATE 30/60s             DELETE 10/60s
LOCATION_UPDATE 240/60s   WAITING_FLAG 10/60s   MAPS_CLIENT_CONFIG 20/60s
ADMIN 60/60s     BULK_OPERATION 3/300s
UPLOAD 5/60s     IMAGE_DELETE 10/60s  NOTIFICATION_CREATE 10/60s
DEFAULT 40/60s   ← silently applied to every withSecurity route that omits `rateLimit:`
2.3 University-wide burst (10 000 students) vs limits
Scenario	Result	Evidence
10 k students behind ONE campus NAT egress IP	L1 (300 req/min) saturates after 0.03 requests/student/min. The entire campus is 429'd within seconds of a class change, while a single remote attacker on a clean IP gets the same 300/min budget. nginx has no limit_req to soften this.	proxy.ts:26-27
10 k students, each touching ≥2 endpoints in a 60 s window	L2's 10 000-entry FIFO cache overflows — every new uid:endpoint key evicts the oldest. At 10 k users × 2 endpoints = 20 k keys, an entry survives ≈ (10 000 / insertion rate) seconds, far less than the 60 s window → counters are reset before they can ever reach 40 or 100 → the per-endpoint limits are effectively not enforced at exactly the scale they were written for. set() also runs the eviction branch when overwriting an existing key, accelerating churn.	rate-limiter.ts:21, 34-41, 54
Distributed deploy (Vercel / >1 container)	L2 and L3 are per-process; each instance holds an independent bucket → effective limit = N × configured limit.	rate-limiter.ts:128-130
Docker prod topology	docker-compose.prod.yml: 1 nextjs container (so one L2 bucket), 2 ws containers (2 independent L4 buckets), env_file: .env for all. Ports bound to 127.0.0.1 except nginx 80/443.	compose:32,63,94,120
Cold start / restart	All in-memory limits (L1,L2,L4,L6,L7,L8) reset.	—
WS burst	nginx ip_hash splits a NAT'd client across ws1/ws2 → each node grants the full 100/10 s, i.e. 2× per IP; and the IP key itself is spoofable (API-04).	nginx:39-41, server/rate-limiter.ts:1-5
Compensating controls present (evidence-only): L1 runs before every other layer and is the only control that constrains unauthenticated traffic; STAGING_MODE/LOAD_TEST_SECRET are unset so the 100 000/min bypass and x-load-test-bypass are inert; L4's uid- and socket-keyed buckets are independent of IP spoofing; role-cache (2 000 entries, 5 min TTL, global-singleton timer) and moderator-permissions cache (1 000 entries, 60 s LRU) are both bounded.
2.4 NAT-shared-IP buckets — summary
Layer	NAT behaviour
L1 (300/min)	shared, and it is the binding constraint — one bucket for the whole campus
L2	not IP-keyed for authenticated routes (uid:pathname) → NAT-agnostic, but broken by cache overflow (§2.3)
L3	disabled
L4	IP bucket shared + spoofable; uid bucket is NAT-agnostic
Spoofability of IP headers	L1/L2-skipAuth use x-real-ip first → nginx overwrites it with $remote_addr (nginx.conf:139, :90) → not spoofable through nginx. It becomes spoofable only if x-real-ip is absent (see UNVERIFIED-2), where L2 falls to XFF-leftmost (attacker-controlled) and L1 falls to XFF-rightmost (safe).
3. FINDINGS
Format: FINDING ID (API-NN) | DOMAIN | SEVERITY | CONFIDENCE | CLASSIFICATION
API-01 (API-01) | RATE LIMITING | HIGH | HIGH | SECURITY VULNERABILITY
Per-endpoint rate limiter cache is smaller than its key space at the stated 10 000-student scale; FIFO eviction silently resets counters, so limits are not enforced under load.
Evidence: rate-limiter.ts:21 (maxSize = 10000), :34-41 (evict-one-oldest-on-every-set, including when the key already exists), :54; key = uid:pathname (:235-237, api-security.ts:248,373-376); ~15 distinct hot endpoints ⇒ ≥150 000 keys at target scale. role-cache.ts/moderator-permissions.ts are correctly bounded; this one is not.
Impact: DEFAULT 40/min, READ 100/min, PAYMENT_CREATE 5/min etc. never trip for legitimate or abusive users once 10 k keys churn; one scripted caller can also flush the table to un-limit another account.
Compensating controls: proxy L1 300/min per IP (proxy.ts:26) still applies — but see API-02; deployment currently single-container.
API-02 (API-02) | RATE LIMITING / PROXY | HIGH | HIGH | SPECIFICATION/BUSINESS-POLICY DECISION (also qualifies as RELIABILITY DEFECT; flagging as the spec decision it must be to avoid deciding it unilaterally)
The only pre-auth control in the system is 300 req/min per IP, which both over-throttles a NAT'd campus and under-throttles a distributed attacker.
Evidence: proxy.ts:25-27, 329-342; no limit_req/limit_conn in nginx/nginx.conf (whole file, 147 lines); RateLimits.* per-endpoint limits are per-uid and unreliable (API-01).
Impact: 10 000 students behind one egress IP ⇒ ~0.03 req/min/student before global 429; conversely a botnet with N IPs gets N×300/min.
Compensating controls: per-uid limits exist and are NAT-agnostic when they work; load-test bypass is disabled (env scan).
API-03 (API-03) | IP TRUST / RATE LIMITING | MEDIUM | HIGH | CONFIRMED PRODUCTION BUG
getClientIp in api-security.ts returns the LEFTMOST X-Forwarded-For hop, contradicting its own comment, its own unit test, and the two other server-side implementations.
Evidence: implementation api-security.ts:120-133, the wrong line is :130 (return parts[0]); comment :126-128 states "Reading from the right (last entry) picks the nearest proxy and lets an attacker spoof their IP" — i.e. the comment argues for parts[0], which is backwards: with nginx appending ($proxy_add_x_forwarded_for), the leftmost entry is the client-supplied one. Test that must fail: src/lib/security/__tests__/security-boundaries-master.test.ts:170-177 imports getClientIp from ../api-security (:13) and asserts XFF 198.51.100.1, 203.0.113.99 → 203.0.113.99; parts[0] yields 198.51.100.1. Divergent implementations: proxy.ts:178 (rightmost), create-first-admin/route.ts:11 (rightmost), server/websocket-server.ts:44 (leftmost).
Impact: wherever x-real-ip is missing, an attacker fully controls the key of ip:<ip>:<route> limiter buckets (api-security.ts:374-376) and can mint unlimited fresh buckets; the security invariant test guarding this cannot pass.
Classification note: CONFIRMED PRODUCTION BUG (IP anti-spoofing regression) and TEST-HARNESS DEFECT (an INV-AUTH-006 test in the "Master Security Boundaries" suite that is asserted but unsatisfiable).
Compensating controls: nginx always sets X-Real-IP $remote_addr (nginx.conf:90, :139) which takes precedence at :121-122, so through nginx the bug is latent; proxy L1 uses the correct rightmost fallback.
API-04 (API-04) | WEBSOCKET / IP TRUST | MEDIUM | HIGH | CONFIRMED PRODUCTION BUG
WebSocket per-IP rate limiting keys are read from the LEFTMOST X-Forwarded-For entry and X-Real-IP is ignored, so PER_IP_LIMIT is fully attacker-controlled.
Evidence: server/websocket-server.ts:43-44 — const clientIp = …xff.split(',')[0]?.trim(); nginx appends the client value first: nginx/nginx.conf:91 proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for while :90 sets X-Real-IP $remote_addr which the WS server never reads. Consumed by server/rate-limiter.ts:14-33 with PER_IP_LIMIT default 100 / 10 s (:1-5).
Impact: rotating X-Forwarded-For: a.b.c.d on the upgrade request bypasses the IP bucket entirely; also combined with ip_hash (nginx:39) a NAT client gets 2× quota across ws1/ws2.
Compensating controls: PER_USER_LIMIT (uid-keyed, default 200/10 s) and PER_SOCKET_LIMIT (60/10 s) are unaffected by IP spoofing; STAGING_MODE is unset so the RFC1918 / 100 000 bypass is inert; the socket is bound to 127.0.0.1 in compose (only reachable via nginx).
API-05 (API-05) | CORS / ORIGIN | MEDIUM | HIGH | SECURITY VULNERABILITY
Two same-named origin validators with opposite policies; the per-route one allows any *.vercel.app / *.adtu.in, reflects it as Access-Control-Allow-Origin together with Allow-Credentials: true, and never emits Vary: Origin.
Evidence: permissive api-security.ts:202-226, wildcard at :220 (hostname.endsWith('.vercel.app') || hostname.endsWith('.adtu.in')), ACAO+credentials at :426-430; strict proxy.ts:232-239. Preflight api-security.ts:251-265 returns Access-Control-Allow-Origin: allowOrigin || '*' with Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true (spec-invalid pairing) for any origin that fails the check, plus Max-Age: 86400. Test evidence: security-boundaries-master.test.ts:17-20 asserts the proxy function rejects https://attacker-domain.vercel.app — the identically-named api-security function returns true for it.
Impact: any page served from any *.vercel.app project can read any withSecurity API response cross-origin when it presents a token; disallowed origins receive a preflight that still succeeds for non-credentials:'include' fetches carrying Authorization; absent Vary: Origin makes any future caching layer an origin-reflection oracle.
Compensating controls: proxy runs the strict validator first for POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE (proxy.ts:354-358) so state-changing CSRF is protected; API auth uses Authorization, not cookies, so a cookie-only attacker gains nothing; withSecurity forces Cache-Control: no-store (:422); non-wrapped routes emit no ACAO at all.
API-06 (API-06) | CORS / PREFLIGHT | LOW | HIGH | CONSISTENCY DEFECT
Preflight is answered before auth and before the route is even reached, advertises all methods and a 24 h Max-Age, and falls back to ACAO: * when Origin is absent or unparseable.
Evidence: api-security.ts:250-265 (origin = header || '*' → allowOrigin = … || '*').
Impact: over-broad preflight caching (86 400 s) means a browser that once received a permissive preflight will not re-negotiate for a day; ACAO: * + Allow-Credentials: true is rejected by browsers for credentialed requests but accepted for plain ones.
Compensating controls: the actual response ACAO (:426-430) is computed independently and correctly omits the header for disallowed origins, so data is not readable; Allow-Headers omits x-load-test-bypass, x-internal-token, X-Alertmanager-Token.
API-07 (API-07) | RATE LIMITING PLACEMENT | MEDIUM | HIGH | RELIABILITY DEFECT
Rate limiting runs after token verification and role resolution, so unauthenticated and bad-token floods consume no budget while each attempt costs a Firebase verifyIdToken round-trip; schema validation runs after the limiter, so malformed payloads consume the victim's budget.
Evidence: order at api-security.ts:317-370 (auth) → :372-392 (limiter) → :394-408 (schema); verifyApiAuth has no limiter at all (api-auth.ts:57-156); 81 routes use neither wrapper nor limiter.
Impact: cheap credential-stuffing / amplification against Google's verify endpoint and the role cache; an attacker can 429 a legitimate user by sending garbage to the same uid:path (only possible with their token) — more importantly, the pre-auth flood itself is unbounded except by L1.
Compensating controls: proxy L1 300/min per IP runs first for every request.
API-08 (API-08) | COVERAGE | MEDIUM-HIGH | HIGH | CONSISTENCY DEFECT
81 of 180 API routes have no rate limiting of any kind, 12 have no authentication mechanism of any kind, and 80 routes silently inherit DEFAULT 40/min by omitting rateLimit:.
Evidence (computed over all 180 src/app/api/**/route.ts):
- 81 routes contain neither withSecurity nor checkRateLimit/applyRateLimit — including admin/reassign-students… (correction: that one does use withSecurity; the list is the 81 computed in §4).
- 12 routes contain no auth helper at all: admin/alerts, buses/[id]/view, check-first-user, create-first-admin, faculties, get-bus-fee, health, health/db, landing-video, metrics, payment/webhook/razorpay, routes/[id]/view. Nine are deliberately public; /api/metrics is not (API-09) and /api/admin/alerts is documented as internal-but-isn't (API-19).
- withSecurity used by 80 routes; ~18 of those pass no schema (admin/dashboard-counts, admin/integrity-scan, analytics, applications/[id], auth/user, driver/available-buses, driver/check-active-trip, driver/dashboard-data, drivers/[id]/status, moderators/[id]/status, notifications, notifications/[id], notifications/read, payment/export, payment/transactions, student/dashboard-data, unauth-users/create) → raw body + query merge reaches the handler unvalidated (api-security.ts:395).
Impact: authorization and validation correctness depend entirely on per-route discipline.
Compensating controls: proxy L1; many manual routes do implement requireModeratorPermission/role checks correctly.
API-09 (API-09) | INFORMATION DISCLOSURE / RESOURCE | MEDIUM | HIGH | SECURITY VULNERABILITY
/api/metrics is unauthenticated, unrate-limited, opens a raw TCP connection to Redis and issues AUTH+INFO on every request, and is not covered by nginx's IP restriction.
Evidence: src/app/api/metrics/route.ts:14-51 (probeRedis, 1 200 ms socket, AUTH <password> written to the socket), :63,80-97 (emits process_cpu_seconds_total, process_resident_memory_bytes, nodejs_heap_*, redis_memory_used_bytes, redis_commands_total, itms_buses_total), :104-110 (200, no-store). nginx/nginx.conf:121-132 restricts /metrics only, not /api/metrics. The route performs no auth and no checkRateLimit. src/app/api/metrics/json/route.ts:16-19 is correctly gated to ['admin','moderator'] — showing the intent.
Impact: unlimited (up to L1's 300/min/IP) unauthenticated forcing of Redis TCP connections and CPU work; process/heap/CPU/fleet disclosure that sizes a DoS.
Compensating controls: the Redis password is used on the socket but never echoed into the response; Cache-Control: no-store; Redis is on the internal Docker network only.
API-10 (API-10) | PROXY / SPECIFICATION | MEDIUM-HIGH | HIGH | SPECIFICATION–IMPLEMENTATION CONTRADICTION
src/proxy.ts documents authentication and role-based access control at the edge; the implementation never blocks an unauthenticated API request.
Evidence: claims at proxy.ts:4-12 ("Authentication verification via Firebase session cookies", "Role-based access control for protected routes"); implementation proxy.ts:375-391 — step 10 is literally an empty comment block and step 11 only sets response.headers.set('X-Proxy-Auth', 'none') with the comment "Don't block — the API handler will do final auth."
Impact: the security review of this file would conclude the edge enforces auth; it does not. Every unprotected route (API-08, API-09, API-19) is therefore world-reachable. No test asserts edge auth enforcement.
Compensating controls: X-Proxy-Auth is set but no route reads it (grep: zero consumers).
API-11 (API-11) | INPUT VALIDATION | MEDIUM | HIGH | CONFIRMED PRODUCTION BUG
Math.min(parseInt(param), cap) returns NaN for non-numeric input, defeating the cap and propagating NaN into SQL LIMIT, Firestore limit()/offset(), and slice arithmetic → HTTP 500.
Evidence:
Route	Line	Expression	?limit=abc result
applications/all	:25	Math.min(parseInt(…), 200)	NaN → getAllPaginated(NaN, offset) (application.service.ts:100)
students	:55	Math.min(parseInt(…), MAX_LIMIT)	NaN
admin/audit-logs	:71	Math.min(parseInt(…), 50)	NaN
reassignment-logs	:63	Math.min(…,100) + Math.max(…,0)	NaN
notifications	:21-22	Math.min(Math.max(parseInt(…),1),100)	Math.max(NaN,1)=NaN → Math.min(NaN,100)=NaN → findByUser(uid, NaN)
payment/export	:21-22	parseInt(startYearStr) with no range check	NaN → new Date(Date.UTC(NaN-1,…)) → Invalid Date
payment/transactions	:19-20	no cap at all	NaN or unbounded (API-12)
Impact: trivially triggered 500s on authenticated endpoints, several of which have no per-route rate limit (API-08); defeats every "bounded pagination" cap.	 	 	 
Compensating controls: all affected routes require auth; admin/audit-logs/students/applications/all cap the value correctly when numeric.	 	 	 
API-12 (API-12) | RESOURCE / INPUT VALIDATION | MEDIUM | HIGH | PERFORMANCE/RESOURCE DEFECT
Two list endpoints accept completely uncapped limit/page, turning one request into an unbounded read into process memory.
Evidence: src/app/api/feedback/route.ts:222-224 page = parseInt(searchParams.get('page')), limit = parseInt(searchParams.get('limit')) → readFeedbackPaginated(page, limit) → feedback-utils.ts:24-40 Firestore .limit(limit).offset((page-1)*limit).get() — ?limit=1000000 materialises the collection; src/app/api/payment/transactions/route.ts:19-20 page/limit with no cap → payment.service.ts slice(offset, offset + pageSize) and a SQL LIMIT.
Impact: single-request memory/CPU exhaustion and multi-second DB scans; page=0/negative yields negative Firestore offset.
Compensating controls: both require auth; proxy L1 300/min; contrast with correctly capped admin/audit-logs (≤50), applications/all (≤200), students (≤200), reassignment-logs (≤100), notifications (≤100).
API-13 (API-13) | ERROR SEMANTICS | MEDIUM-HIGH | HIGH | RELIABILITY DEFECT
The error contract is inconsistent across the surface: raw error.message in production 500 bodies, HTTP 200 carrying failure, and empty catch blocks that swallow security-relevant errors.
Evidence:
- Raw messages returned to clients: notifications/[id]/route.ts:114,148; cron/cleanup-trip-history/route.ts:30,43; fleet/assign-drivers/route.ts:72,108,189; fleet/assign-routes/route.ts:57,92,161; metrics/json/route.ts:29 ({error: err.message}); admin/export-firestore path also returns error.message.
- 200-on-failure: landing-video/route.ts:37-40 returns {success:true, url:''}; admin/firestore-health:169-175; admin/alerts returns 200 + ok:false; payment/recover:351 returns 200 + verification_pending during Razorpay outage; settings/landing-config falls back on error.
- Swallowed/empty catches: admin/simulate-deadlines:194,216 (catch (e) {}); metrics:23,28; admin/update-user:120; waiting-flag/acknowledge:197.
- 401/500 confusion: applications/save-draft/route.ts:20-27 lets verifyIdToken throw into the outer catch → invalid token yields 500 "Failed to save draft" instead of 401.
- Only 17 / 180 routes reference safeErrorMessage/handleApiError.
Impact: DB/driver/URL internals leak to clients; monitoring sees success where there was failure; mobile clients cannot distinguish auth failure from server failure.
Compensating controls: withSecurity itself is correct (api-security.ts:434-447 → "An internal error occurred" in production); safe-error.ts SAFE_ERROR_PATTERNS exists and is used by 17 routes; next.config.ts removeConsole: {exclude:['error','warn']} keeps server logs intact.
API-14 (API-14) | VALIDATION ORDER | LOW-MEDIUM | HIGH | CONSISTENCY DEFECT
Several routes parse and validate the body before verifying the token; several authenticate but never validate.
Evidence: applications/approve/route.ts:9-15 — token presence (:9) → await request.json() (:12) → applicationId check (:13) → verifyIdToken (:15): a garbage Bearer token still forces full body buffering (up to 10 MB, API-15) before rejection. Same shape: applications/reject, applications/approve-unauth:28-31, feedback/route.ts:26 (parses before any auth, accepts body.idToken), applications/save-draft:12-27 (auth but no role check, no schema, no rate limit), students/add:40 (auth → rate limit → parse — correct order, contrast).
Impact: pre-auth work amplification; save-draft accepts arbitrary formData JSON persisted verbatim.
Compensating controls: no IDOR — ownership is enforced in the domain layer (application.service.ts:92 existing.applicantUid !== uid) and a state machine guards immutable states (:96-102); nginx client_max_body_size 10M; proxy L1.
API-15 (API-15) | BODY SIZE / MEMORY | MEDIUM | HIGH | PERFORMANCE/RESOURCE DEFECT
*The 256 KB body cap exists only inside withSecurity; the other 100 routes accept up to nginx's 10 MB with no cap, and the cap that does exist measures UTF-16 units rather than bytes.*
Evidence: cap at api-security.ts:40, 139-159 (checks content-length and text.length — :151 counts UTF-16 code units, so multi-byte UTF-8 can reach 768 KB under a lying/absent content-length); global nginx/nginx.conf:10 client_max_body_size 10M; next.config.ts serverActions.bodySizeLimit: '10mb'. Routes calling request.json()/request.text() with no cap: fleet/assign-drivers, fleet/assign-routes, applications/submit|approve|reject|save-draft, buses/create|update, routes/[id]/update, settings/system-config, moderators/[id]/update, students/[id]/update, renew-services, renewal-requests/*, tokenRefresh, admin/set-driver-reserved, admin/run-session-activation, payment/webhook/razorpay:19 (await request.text() then HMAC over the whole body).
Impact: up-to-10 MB buffering per concurrent request on non-wrapped routes; unauthenticated HMAC-over-10 MB on the Razorpay webhook (only bounded by L1's 300/min/IP).
Compensating controls: nginx's 10 MB is a hard ceiling; withSecurity routes are correctly limited to 256 KB and return 413 (:289-294).
API-16 (API-16) | MASS ASSIGNMENT | MEDIUM | MEDIUM-HIGH | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT
Two state-changing routes write client JSON straight into the database with no allow-list and no schema, while sibling routes implement the pattern correctly.
Evidence: routes/[id]/update/route.ts:27-33 — const updatedRouteData = await request.json(); const updated = {...updatedRouteData, totalStops: …, updatedAt: …} — every client key (id, routeId, createdAt, stops, …) is spread into the update; stops is only tested with Array.isArray for totalStops. settings/system-config/route.ts:66-79 — {...oldConfig, ...config} with only if (!rawConfig) as validation; mapProvider is force-set but nothing else is constrained. Correct pattern for comparison: students/[id]/update/route.ts:26-45 (ALLOWED_FIELDS + BLOCKED_FIELDS + normalizeShift enum) and moderators/[id]/update/route.ts:16-31 (ALLOWED_FIELDS + BLOCKED_FIELDS logging blocked attempts).
Impact: an admin/moderator (or moderator holding routes.canEdit) can persist unexpected columns; parseInt(capacity) (buses/create) accepts NaN/negative with no range check.
Compensating controls: both routes require admin/moderator auth + requireModeratorPermission('routes','canEdit'); withSecurity routes get zod .strip() of unknown keys by default.
API-17 (API-17) | HEADERS | LOW-MEDIUM | HIGH | CONSISTENCY DEFECT
Three layers emit security headers with contradictory values, so responses carry duplicate, conflicting headers.
Evidence:
Header	next.config.ts	proxy.ts	nginx/nginx.conf
Strict-Transport-Security	max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains; preload (prod only)	—	max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains; preload (:29)
X-Frame-Options	SAMEORIGIN	SAMEORIGIN (:349)	DENY (:31)
Permissions-Policy	camera=(self), microphone=(), geolocation=(self), payment=(self), …	—	geolocation=(self), camera=(), microphone=() (:33)
X-Content-Type-Options	nosniff	nosniff (:348)	nosniff (:30) → up to 3×
Referrer-Policy	strict-origin-when-cross-origin	—	same value (:32) → 2×
Also: CSP frame-ancestors 'self' https://accounts.google.com https://*.firebaseapp.com https://adtu-itms.vercel.app (next.config.ts) permits framing by any Firebase-Hosting project, which conflicts with both X-Frame-Options values; script-src carries 'unsafe-eval'; production connect-src includes ws://* wss://*. No Vary: Origin anywhere while ACAO is origin-reflecting (API-05).	 	 	 
Impact: unpredictable effective policy (browsers differ on duplicate XFO/Permissions-Policy handling); operators reading nginx think DENY applies while the app asserts SAMEORIGIN.	 	 	 
Compensating controls: all variants block cross-origin framing, so clickjacking is denied under any interpretation; object-src 'none', base-uri 'self', form-action are correct.	 	 	 
API-18 (API-18) | ORIGIN ALLOWLIST CONFIG | MEDIUM | HIGH | SPECIFICATION/BUSINESS-POLICY DECISION
A production deployment built from the shipped .env treats http://localhost:3000 as a trusted origin, and Server Actions ship a non-dev-gated allow-list containing localhost:3000 and *.devtunnels.ms.
Evidence: .env NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL=http://localhost:3000 (.env.docker identical; .env.example shows the intended https://your-production-domain.vercel.app); docker-compose.prod.yml:40,71,101 uses env_file: .env with NODE_ENV=production. Consumed unconditionally by api-security.ts:181-200 (hosts.add(new URL(appUrl).host) — the NODE_ENV==='development' guard at :188 covers only the explicit localhost entries) and by proxy.ts:214-230 (STATIC_ALLOWED_ORIGINS includes NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL before the dev guard). next.config.ts experimental.serverActions.allowedOrigins lists localhost:3000 and *.devtunnels.ms with no environment gate.
Impact: http://localhost:3000 passes both CSRF origin checks and is reflected as ACAO in production; *.devtunnels.ms is an accepted Server-Action origin in production. Practical exploitability is low (the attacker must serve content from the victim's own machine / a Microsoft dev tunnel).
Compensating controls: https://adtu-itms.vercel.app is also pinned (proxy.ts:217, api-security.ts:187); ALLOWED_ORIGINS is unset so no extra hosts are added; CORS only matters where ACAO is emitted (API-05).
API-19 (API-19) | WEBHOOK / DEPLOYMENT CONFIG | MEDIUM | HIGH | SPECIFICATION–IMPLEMENTATION CONTRADICTION
/api/admin/alerts documents an nginx RFC-1918 restriction that does not exist, and its required AM_WEBHOOK_SECRET is absent from every env file, so the endpoint fails closed with 500 in production.
Evidence: doc comment admin/alerts/route.ts:7-10 — "Restricted to internal network via Nginx (RFC-1918 ranges only -- deny external)"; code :36-41 returns 500 in production when AM_WEBHOOK_SECRET is unset; nginx/nginx.conf has no location /api/admin/alerts (only /health and /metrics are IP-restricted at :104-132; location / at :135 proxies the webhook publicly); env key scan — AM_WEBHOOK_SECRET is not among the 52 keys in .env, 50 in .env.example, or 49 in .env.docker.
Impact: the documented Alertmanager → in-app admin notification path cannot function in the shipped configuration, and the endpoint is a public, unrate-limited 500 (see API-08).
Compensating controls: the HMAC comparison that is implemented is sound (:45-50, both sides HMAC'd with timingSafeEqual, fixed-length digests); fail-closed is the correct posture for a missing secret; the body is parsed only after the secret check.
API-20 (API-20) | RATE LIMITING / DISTRIBUTED | MEDIUM | HIGH | SECURITY VULNERABILITY
The distributed limiter is dead code and its fallback is fail-open, so all limits are per-process and reset on restart or cold start.
Evidence: rate-limiter.ts:61-62 reads UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL/UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN — neither key exists in .env (52 keys), .env.example (50) or .env.docker (49); :128-130 falls back to local memory when unset; :158-160 catch → return checkRateLimit(...) (silent fail-open on any fetch error) with no logging or metric. docker-compose.prod.yml sets REDIS_URL (:38,69,99) which the HTTP limiter never reads. output: process.env.VERCEL ? undefined : 'standalone' (next.config.ts) confirms a serverless path exists where N instances ⇒ N× the limit.
Impact: on any multi-instance deploy the advertised AUTH 10/min, LOGIN 5/min, PAYMENT_CREATE 5/min are multiplied by instance count; a Redis outage silently degrades distributed limits to per-process limits with no signal.
Compensating controls: docker-compose.prod.yml runs exactly one nextjs container, so the Docker topology is single-process; proxy L1 is also per-process but the same single container applies; role invalidation does use REDIS_URL pub/sub correctly with a documented best-effort fallback (role-cache.ts:138-144).
API-21 (API-21) | RATE LIMITER MEMORY | LOW | MEDIUM-HIGH | RELIABILITY DEFECT
A second, unbounded rate-limit store exists alongside the bounded one, and the shared limiter's cleanup timer is not singleton-guarded or unref()ed.
Evidence: feedback-utils.ts:251 const rateLimitStore = new Map<string, number>() with :253-273 — no TTL, no eviction, no cleanup pass, no size cap (comment at :250 acknowledges "In-memory is reset on server restart… Redis is better but out of scope"); contrast rate-limiter.ts:54-59 (bounded 10 000 + 5 min cleanup) and the correct singleton pattern at role-cache.ts:44-52, 61-66 (globalThis.__roleCacheCleanupStarted guards) — rate-limiter.ts:57-59 has no such guard and no .unref().
Build evidence: strings UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL, createRateLimitId, X-RateLimit-Reset each appear in 72–88 separate files under .next/server/chunks/, whereas role-cache appears in 2 — see UNVERIFIED-3 for whether that implies multiple module instances (⇒ multiple setIntervals).
Impact: feedback's store grows one entry per distinct submitting uid forever; a duplicated RateLimitCache would multiply timers and split the 10 000-key budget across instances (worsening API-01).
Compensating controls: RateLimitCache is itself bounded at 10 000; proxy cache bounded at 50 000; WS limiter bounded at 10 000 per map with an .unref()ed timer (server/rate-limiter.ts:22-27, 48-51).
API-22 (API-22) | TOKEN HANDLING | LOW | MEDIUM-HIGH | CONSISTENCY DEFECT
Body tokens (idToken) are accepted by default on every withSecurity route and are actively sent by client code; they are removed from context.body only when a schema was supplied.
Evidence: default allowBodyToken = true (api-security.ts:236); extraction :105-107; strip happens only inside if (schema) at :397-398, so on the ~18 schema-less routes validatedBody = rawBody (:395) still contains idToken and is handed to the handler. ~36 routes restate allowBodyToken: true explicitly; only withWebhookSecurity sets false (:520) — and that wrapper is unused. Client call sites actively sending tokens in bodies: src/app/student/track-bus/page.tsx:932, src/components/FeedbackModal.tsx:57, src/app/student/bus/page.tsx:157,193, src/app/driver/students/page.tsx:207, src/app/driver/profile-requests/page.tsx:84,114, src/lib/dataService.ts:126,240. validation-schemas.ts:197 even declares idToken: z.string().optional() inside GenerateBusPassSchema, so it survives validation there.
Impact: an ID token can be persisted or logged by any schema-less handler; request bodies are the most commonly captured artifact (APM, error reporting, proxy body logging); the same token accepted in two transport locations doubles the places a secret can leak.
Compensating controls: query-string idToken is explicitly rejected (api-security.ts:304-311, idToken key skipped when merging query params); CSRF origin checks still require the attacker to already know the token, so body acceptance is not a CSRF bypass; webhook wrapper correctly disables it.
API-23 (API-23) | VALIDATION ERROR DISCLOSURE | LOW | HIGH | CONSISTENCY DEFECT
Zod failure paths and messages are returned verbatim in HTTP 400 bodies to any authenticated caller, exposing internal field names and schema structure.
Evidence: validation-schemas.ts validateInput builds  `Validation failed: ${issues.join(', ')}`  where each issue is  `${i.path.join('.')}: ${i.message}` ; consumed and returned unmodified at api-security.ts:400-405.
Impact: an authenticated user can enumerate field names, nesting, and the custom messages of every schema in the system (57 exported schemas, validation-schemas.ts:14-582); also logged to stderr on every failure (validateInput console.error, suppressed only under NODE_ENV==='test').
Compensating controls: zod's default messages report types, not values, and the schemas use bespoke messages for regex constraints; schemas are non-strict by default, so unknown keys are silently stripped rather than named (contrast zod's unrecognized_keys message, which would leak them).
API-24 (API-24) | CSRF | LOW | MEDIUM | SECURITY VULNERABILITY
The mere presence of an x-internal-token header — value never checked, header never consumed anywhere else in the codebase — short-circuits the proxy's CSRF rejection of state-changing requests that carry neither Origin nor Referer.
Evidence: proxy.ts:278-282 (if ((authHeader && authHeader.startsWith('Bearer ')) || internalToken) return true;) immediately before the documented invariant "Reject browser-like state-changing requests without Origin or Referer" at :284-285; grep across src/ and server/ finds x-internal-token in exactly one place — that line.
Impact: weakens a documented control with a knob that appears to authenticate but does not; an attacker can present any value.
Compensating controls: browsers cannot attach custom headers to a simple cross-origin request, and the CORS preflight (api-security.ts:260) does not list x-internal-token, so a browser-based exploit is blocked at preflight; the Authorization: Bearer branch of the same check does require a real, verified token; Origin/Referer are present on essentially all browser POSTs.
API-25 (API-25) | AUTHORIZATION DEFENCE-IN-DEPTH | LOW | MEDIUM-HIGH | CONSISTENCY DEFECT
Two routes gate on "role is non-empty" rather than "role is in the allowed set", so authorization rests entirely on the single downstream requireModeratorPermission call; and a database read failure is surfaced to users as 403 Insufficient permissions instead of 500.
Evidence: applications/approve/route.ts:33-38 if (!userRole.role) return 403 — passes for any non-empty role including student; applications/approve-unauth/route.ts:44-48 identical; role-cache.ts:109-114 returns {role:'', name:'', employeeId:''} on PostgreSQL read failure (and logs err.message), which withSecurity:363 / verifyApiAuth:126 render as 403 "Insufficient permissions".
Impact: privilege escalation does not occur — verified: requireModeratorPermission (moderator-permissions.ts:74-79) explicitly returns 403 for auth.role !== 'moderator' after allowing admin. But a transient DB outage produces a misleading 403 for legitimate admins, and any future refactor that drops the permission call would expose approval to students.
Compensating controls: requireModeratorPermission is correct and present at both call sites; permissionCache is bounded (1 000) with a 60 s TTL and negative-caches unknown moderators to ZERO_MODERATOR_PERMISSIONS (moderator-permissions.ts:24-34).
Additional positive evidence (no finding raised)
Control	Evidence
Razorpay webhook is hardened	payment/webhook/razorpay/route.ts:29-50 — timingSafeEqual with length pre-check, fail-closed on unset RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET (which is present in all env files), :66-70 fetches trusted order details instead of notes, :74-78 integer-paise amount binding, :64-67 502 (not 200) on upstream failure so Razorpay retries
Scanner blocking	proxy.ts:125-157, returns 404 not 403 to avoid fingerprinting (:320-322)
Cron fail-closed + timing-safe	api-auth.ts:183-204, api-security.ts:466-490
Prototype-pollution guard	src/lib/security/object-safety.ts
SSRF guard on download	src/app/api/download/route.ts uses safeExternalUrl (https-only, host allow-list) + redirect:'error'
tokens-in-URL rejected	api-security.ts:304-311
Secrets not in git	git ls-files → only .env.example tracked; .gitignore:19,25,82-83; git log --all -- .env → empty (never committed)
Role / permission caches bounded	role-cache.ts:24,44-52 (2 000, singleton timer); moderator-permissions.ts:37-39 (1 000, LRU)
Correct allow-list pattern exists	students/[id]/update:26-45, moderators/[id]/update:16-31, students/add:14-27
4. ROUTE HANDLER SAMPLE (30 routes, ≥20 required)
WS = withSecurity; RL = per-route rate limit; Schema = zod option supplied.
#	Route	WS	Auth	RL	Schema	Body cap	Error contract	Notable defect
1	POST /api/applications/submit	–	presence→verifyIdToken→then parse	none	manual typeof	10 MB	500 generic	API-08
2	POST /api/applications/approve	–	presence→parse+validate→verifyIdToken	none	manual if(!id)	10 MB	500 safeErrorMessage	API-14, API-08, API-25
3	POST /api/applications/approve-unauth	–	same as #2	none	asRecord/asString guards	10 MB	500 safeErrorMessage	API-14, API-08, API-25
4	GET /api/applications/get-unauth/[id]	–	verifyIdToken + role	none	none (path)	–	500 generic	API-08
5	POST /api/applications/save-draft	–	verifyIdToken only (no role)	none	none	10 MB	invalid token → 500	API-14, API-13
6	GET /api/applications/all	–	verifyIdToken + role	none	Math.min(…,200)	–	500 generic	API-11
7	GET /api/applications/[id]	✔	admin/moderator	READ 100/min	none	–	plain error	API-08

5. UNVERIFIED ITEMS
1. middleware-manifest.json reports { "middleware": {} } while functions-config-manifest.json registers /_middleware. The proxy is definitively compiled (.next/server/middleware.js → the chunk containing IP_CACHE_MAX), but I could not execute a request to confirm runtime invocation without modifying state. The empty manifest is consistent with Next.js 16's Node-middleware/loadNodeMiddleware path, but treat "proxy runs on every request" as highly-probable, not runtime-proven.
2. Whether x-real-ip is present on every deployment path. nginx sets it (nginx.conf:90, :139) — verified. Vercel's behaviour is not verifiable from the repo. If it is absent there, API-03 becomes immediately live (XFF-leftmost key minting) and API-04's class of bug applies to HTTP too; if present, API-03 is latent.
3. Whether the 72–88 chunk files containing UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL / createRateLimitId / X-RateLimit-Reset represent duplicated module instances at runtime. If they do, each instance registers its own setInterval (rate-limiter.ts:57-59 has no singleton guard) and the 10 000-key budget splits — compounding API-01 and API-21. Not resolvable by static reading of a Turbopack build.
4. Actual numeric values of RATE_LIMIT_PER_USER / RATE_LIMIT_PER_SOCKET in .env (keys present, values not read). RATE_LIMIT_PER_IP and STAGING_MODE/LOAD_TEST_SECRET/ALLOWED_ORIGINS/UPSTASH_*/AM_WEBHOOK_SECRET are confirmed absent.
5. Duplicate-header behaviour in real browsers for X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN + DENY and dual Permissions-Policy (API-17). All interpretations deny cross-origin framing, but the effective Permissions-Policy for camera/geolocation differs by engine.
6. The security test suite's actual pass/fail state. API-03's INV-AUTH-006 rightmost-hop assertion (security-boundaries-master.test.ts:170-177) is unsatisfiable against api-security.ts:130 by code reading alone; I did not run the suite (read-only).
7. CdVXgPlj3fCzm4FRs5Vkyf0 — a malformed key name present in .env and .env.docker but not .env.example (looks like a pasted Firebase-config fragment). Its value and whether any code reads it were not traced.

(2) FINDINGS
APP-01 | P1 | HIGH | CONFIRMED PRODUCTION BUG — Renewal requests permanently blocked by idx_applications_active_student_session.
FILES: renew-service-v2/route.ts, payment.service.ts, application.service.ts:156-241, COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:268-270.
PATH: renew-service-v2:68 builds renewal_<uid>_<day>; submitFinal:177-181 derives startYear = Number(... || 0) (no sessionStartYear in renewal payloads) → session_start_year = 0; mapper copies it verbatim (repository.pg.ts:130-141); upsert throws on error (:373-387) → route catch:100-104 → 503.
INVARIANT: one live application per (applicant, session). CURRENT: every renewal row for a student shares key (applicant_uid, 0); index excludes only rejected/cancelled/expired, so an approved row still holds the key.
SCENARIO: Y1 renewal inserted and approved → row persists with ssy=0 → Y2 renewal (different application_id) INSERT → duplicate key ... idx_applications_active_student_session → 503 forever. Same collision between an offline pending renewal and the online self-heal row online_<paymentId> (payment.service.ts:154-155,381-382, also ssy=0) → payment captured, no application created.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Payment ↔ Application ↔ Identity. USER/DATA: student can never self-renew (student/renew/page.tsx:346); paid renewals can leave no request record.
REMED: give renewals a real session_start_year, or exclude application_type IN ('renewal','renewal_after_soft_block') from the index; restrict the predicate to actionable states; add migration + regression test.
EVIDENCE: schema :268-270; application.service.ts:177-181,241; renew-service-v2:68,71-75,96-104; repository.pg.ts:130-141,373-387.
APP-02 | P1 | HIGH | FINANCIAL INTEGRITY DEFECT — Bulk renewal "idempotency" queries a table that does not exist; extensions are re-applicable.
FILES: renew-services/route.ts:97-113,172-173,226-262; renewal-utils.ts:29-64.
PATH: :103-113 reads payment_ledger where payment_id = 'manual_'+transactionId; no payment_ledger table exists (schema defines payments:274, processed_payments:326, processed_operations:2280); the error is ignored and no code ever inserts manual_*.
INVARIANT: one extension per payment. CURRENT: calculateRenewalDate extends from the current validUntil (renewal-utils.ts:46-55), so each replay adds durationYears again; online mode (:181-195) accepts any historic completed online payment as proof.
SCENARIO: 40-student manual batch replayed after a timeout → all validUntil extended twice (2-8 unbilled service years); no ledger row is written for manual mode at all → loss is invisible to reconciliation.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Payment ↔ Identity entitlement ↔ Seat expiry. USER/DATA: free service years; pushed-out expiry/soft-block dates; revenue mismatch.
REMED: use existing processed_payments/processed_operations claims keyed by transactionId|studentUid|durationYears, or make the write idempotent on last_processed_application_id.
EVIDENCE: renew-services/route.ts:103-113,173,197-220,244-268; schema has no payment_ledger (all CREATE TABLE at :78-2280).
APP-03 | P1 | MEDIUM-HIGH | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT — Approval is a 3-step, non-idempotent sequence; capacity+identity commit before the application is finalized.
FILES: application.service.ts:316-548; schema :752-829,900-969.
PATH: claim :325 → identity_activate_student_with_capacity :462 (commits capacity+student) → finalize_application_approval :511; failure → catch:535-545 releases the lease and returns 500. The capacity RPC has no idempotency predicate; cleanup_abandoned_application_locks (schema:1037) has zero callers.
INVARIANT: a seat counts for exactly one live student; approval is all-or-nothing.
SCENARIO: (a) server fetch abort (10 s AbortSignal, supabase-server.ts:41-44) or bus row locked by a long reassign_students_atomically batch → capacity committed, client sees error → retry → +2 loads for 1 student; (b) crash after step 2 → lease expires → re-claim → same; (c) step 2 commits, finalize fails → moderator clicks Reject (:579 succeeds, state still submitted) → application deleted + payment rejected while the student already exists active and counted.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ Identity ↔ Payment ↔ Audit. USER/DATA: phantom seats ("full" bus nobody rides) or an approved student with a rejected payment.
REMED: fold the three steps into one RPC (pattern exists: approve_renewal_with_seat), make the capacity RPC skip when last_processed_application_id = p_application_id, and never release the lease on a partial commit; block reject once a student has been created.
EVIDENCE: application.service.ts:325,462-479,511-525,535-545,579,634; schema :752-829 (no idempotency check), :838-853.
APP-04 | P1 | MEDIUM | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT — renewal_after_soft_block is never created, so the seat-reclaim branch is unreachable.
FILES: application.service.ts:405-437; renew-service-v2/route.ts:96-98; payment.service.ts:189,412; schema :1711-1850,932-959.
PATH: only submitFinal(..., {applicationType:'renewal'}) creates renewal rows; grep shows no creator of renewal_after_soft_block. The reclaim branch (:405) requires exactly that type.
INVARIANT: status='active' ⇒ the student's seat is counted.
SCENARIO: seat-released student renews → standard path → finalize_application_approval (schema:932-947) sets active + extends validity, does not increment capacity and does not clear seat_released_at → occupiesSeat() false (admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:69-75) → bus filled to capacity plus this rider (physical overbooking); the next expiry cycle decrements a seat never re-incremented → permanent undercount.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ Identity ↔ expiry cron. USER/DATA: over-subscribed buses; active-but-uncounted rider; reconcile "corrects" the wrong direction.
REMED: branch on seatWasReleased regardless of application_type, or emit renewal_after_soft_block in renew-service-v2 when seat_released_at IS NOT NULL.
EVIDENCE: application.service.ts:396,405; renew-service-v2:96-98; schema :932-947.
CAP-01 | P1 | MEDIUM | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT — Cron reconciliation is read-then-write and erases concurrent capacity increments.
FILES: admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:87,105-117,128-159; callers cron/cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:397, cron/integrity-sweep/route.ts:41.
PATH: student snapshot (:105-117) and counter write (:155-159) are separate non-transactional statements, no lock, both crons run with dryRun:false + alertOnLargeDelta:true.
INVARIANT: morning_load/evening_load = Σ seats owned.
SCENARIO: cron reads (N=27) → moderator approves (:462 sets 28 + creates student) → cron writes 27 → one real rider uncounted → next approvals can reach capacity+1 before the atomic guard trips. Reverse interleaving creates a phantom seat and fires the largeDelta alert.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Application approval ↔ Seat ↔ Fleet. USER/DATA: overbooked buses or false "capacity full".
REMED: recount+write in one statement with SELECT … FOR UPDATE on the bus (as schema:2249-2261 does); include current_members in the same write.
EVIDENCE: admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:105-117,155-159; both cron callers (line numbers above).
CAP-02 | P2 | MEDIUM | CONSISTENCY DEFECT — Session activation: three non-atomic writes and a 5-minute lease that is never re-validated.
FILES: session-activation.service.ts:243-246,300-342,400,421,444,475,482; schema :2109-2129.
PATH: claim → incrementBusCapacity :314 → createStudent :329 → remove(appId) :342; the app row write checks processing_lock nowhere (contrast schema:910-916); compensation exists only for the student write (:335-337).
INVARIANT: one activation per application; one counted seat per activated student.
SCENARIO: an activation stalls >5 min (10 s timeouts, lock contention) → a second cron/admin run re-claims the expired lease → both increment → 2 loads for 1 student; or remove(appId) throws after capacity+student committed → next run re-activates → +1 per retry.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ Application ↔ Identity. USER/DATA: phantom seats, duplicate activation audits.
REMED: one RPC for capacity+student+app-consumption, or re-validate the lease inside each write and treat "already activated" as success.
EVIDENCE: session-activation.service.ts:245,314,335-342; schema :2109-2129.
CAP-03 | P2 | MEDIUM | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT — Seat-reclaimed renewal: increment then update, no compensation.
FILE: renew-services/route.ts:226-262.
PATH: incrementBusCapacity :244 commits → updateStudent :246; catch :250-260 only records a failure string.
INVARIANT: capacity increment ⇒ the student record is updated in the same unit of work.
SCENARIO: update fails after the increment → "Renewal failed during seat reclamation" → counter +1 forever; admin retries → +1 again and validUntil extended again (compounds APP-02).
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ Identity ↔ Payment. USER/DATA: bus appears fuller than it is; repeated retries inflate counters and entitlements.
REMED: single RPC performing capacity check+increment+student update+application finalize (mirror approve_renewal_with_seat).
EVIDENCE: renew-services/route.ts:239-260.
APP-05 | P2 | HIGH | RELIABILITY DEFECT — Reassignment rollback snapshot: retry and failure detection are dead code.
FILE: admin/reassign-students/route.ts:210,236-337.
PATH: reassignStudentsAtomically commits at :210, then await supabase.from('reassignment_logs').insert([...]) :290 sets auditWritten = true :300 unconditionally — the catch (retryErr) :302 never fires because postgrest resolves {error} unless throwOnError (node_modules/@supabase/postgrest-js/src/PostgrestBuilder.ts:390,563; no throwOnError in supabase-server.ts:35-44).
INVARIANT: every committed reassignment has a reassignment_logs row (rollback precondition, schema:2351-2361).
SCENARIO: insert fails (10 s fetch timeout) after the RPC committed → route returns success:true with an operationId → POST /admin/rollback-reassignment 404s → the change is irreversible with no detectable gap; the reassignment_rollback_snapshot_failed audit (:318-336) can never be written.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ Audit/rollback ↔ Smart-allocation UI ("Undo" snackbar). USER/DATA: silent permanent reassignments.
REMED: read res.error each attempt, set auditWritten from !res.error, fail the request when no log row exists, or write the log inside the same RPC.
EVIDENCE: reassign-students/route.ts:287-311; PostgrestBuilder.ts:390,563; supabase-server.ts:35-44.
CAP-04 | P2 | MEDIUM | RELIABILITY DEFECT — No deterministic bus lock ordering in reassignment and rollback.
FILE: COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:2183-2246,2364-2435.
PATH: each plan locks student_profiles FOR UPDATE → bus_decrement_capacity(from) → bus_increment_capacity(to) in plan array order; rollback locks log → students → buses, unordered.
INVARIANT: concurrent multi-bus transactions cannot deadlock (or fail fast with a clear error).
SCENARIO: op1 = A→B and op2 = B→A run concurrently → one aborts 40P01 → the whole batch (up to 80 students, reassignment-service.ts:17) fails and is retried; repeated retries widen the missing-log window of APP-05.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ Smart-allocation ↔ Driver/route. USER/DATA: intermittent opaque 500s on reassignment.
REMED: SELECT … FOR UPDATE all involved buses (and student ids) in ORDER BY id before the loop.
EVIDENCE: schema:2197-2231,2369-2435; batch size reassignment-service.ts:17,26-38.
CAP-05 | P2 | MEDIUM | CONSISTENCY DEFECT — current_members: code says GENERATED/read-only, schema defines a plain column, three writers disagree.
FILES: schema :179,804,1122,1145,1778,2251,2425; fleet.repository.pg.ts:28,62,199-210; admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:155-159; sync-bus-counts/route.ts:62-63.
INVARIANT: current_members = morning_load + evening_load.
CURRENT: schema = plain INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0; code asserts GENERATED ALWAYS AS (…) STORED … READ ONLY (fleet.repository.pg.ts:28, admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:155) so reconcile never writes it — while every RPC assigns it and sync-bus-counts writes it via updateBus.
SCENARIO: if plain → reconcile fixes morning/evening but leaves current_members stale forever, and all current_members readers (fleet/live-status, driver/dashboard-data, profile-service) report wrong occupancy; if generated → every explicit current_members = assignment (schema:1122,1145) makes capacity RPCs fail outright. The two artifacts cannot both be true.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Fleet ↔ Seat ↔ Driver/Student UI. USER/DATA: wrong seat numbers or total capacity-write outage.
REMED: reconcile migration and schema; delete explicit assignments if generated, otherwise have reconcile write it.
EVIDENCE: schema:179 vs fleet.repository.pg.ts:28,62 vs admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:155-159 vs sync-bus-counts/route.ts:63.
CAP-06 | P2 | MEDIUM | CONSISTENCY DEFECT — Two incompatible definitions of "occupies a seat" → counters oscillate.
FILES: schema:2249-2261,2423-2435 vs admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:69-75,107-117.
CURRENT: reassign/rollback recount counts only sp.status='active'; the canonical reconciler counts active plus soft_blocked/pending_deletion without seat_released_at, and keys buses by bus_id = b.id OR b.bus_number (RPCs, schema:1109,1141) whereas reconcile keys only by id (:98-110).
SCENARIO: a bus with one soft-blocked, seat-held student is touched by a reassignment → recount drops them (frees a seat) → the daily reconciler restores them → largeDelta alert fires and the last seat alternately allows/denies.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ expiry cron ↔ Reassignment. USER/DATA: spurious discrepancy alerts; flapping capacity guard.
REMED: one SQL predicate (e.g. seat_occupies(sp)) used by both, and id-or-number bus matching in the reconciler.
EVIDENCE: schema:2257-2259,2432 vs admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:69-75,109-110.
CAP-07 | P2 | MEDIUM | CONSISTENCY DEFECT — Soft-block × renewal TOCTOU inside soft_block_student_with_seat_release.
FILES: schema:1572-1651; cron/cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:227-257 (RPC at :250).
INVARIANT: a just-renewed student is never soft-blocked.
CURRENT: the RPC locks the student and only checks status='active'; the expiry decision is computed in TS from an earlier read and the row is never re-validated against valid_until/soft_block.
SCENARIO: cron reads S → moderator approves S's renewal (extends validUntil) → the RPC runs, sees active, decrements capacity, sets soft_blocked with the stale pre-computed timestamp → a paying, just-renewed student is blocked and loses the seat.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ renewal/Application ↔ expiry cron. USER/DATA: paid student locked out until manual reactivation; spurious seat release.
REMED: recompute the block predicate inside the RPC from the locked row (return "not due" rather than forcing); pass no pre-computed timestamps.
EVIDENCE: schema:1587-1634; cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:239-257.
CAP-08 | P2 | MEDIUM | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT — Student deletion decrements after the cascade delete, swallows failures, and audits a decrement that may not have happened.
FILES: cleanup-helpers.ts:89,94,190-199; admin/delete-student/route.ts:22,58; schema :1912-1965.
CURRENT: delete_student_cascade_v1 deletes profile+applications+user and performs no capacity update; the decrement is a separate later call whose failure is only logged; the route's audit always records seatDecremented: shouldDecrement (a computed boolean, not an observed outcome).
SCENARIO: cascade commits → timeout/restart before :192 → counter stays at N for N-1 riders permanently, and the audit claims the seat was released, corrupting later delta attribution; a crash between getStudentById and cascade leaves a stale shouldDecrement in the audit.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Identity deletion ↔ Seat ↔ Audit. USER/DATA: phantom seats; misleading audit during investigations.
REMED: decrement inside delete_student_cascade_v1 (same TX, using the locked row's shift); record the RPC's actual result.
EVIDENCE: cleanup-helpers.ts:89-100,190-199; delete-student/route.ts:22,58; schema :1940-1950.
APP-06 | P2 | MEDIUM | SERVER-SIDE MISSING VALIDATION — The Phase-2 eligibility gate (eligibleApproval = softBlock+1d) is entirely inert, and the "future" classifier is anchor-blind.
FILES: application-eligibility.ts:192-243,112-118,151-185; application.service.ts:177-189,236,342-354.
INVARIANT: a next-session application is not approvable until outgoing seats are freed (documented at types/application.ts:43,218 and application-eligibility.ts:8,125-126: "enforced server-side in both approval routes").
CURRENT (three independent breaks): (1) deriveCreationCategorisation/resolveEligibleApprovalAtCreation/computeFutureEligibleApproval have zero callers outside their own file (grep) → eligible_approval is never stored server-side (only body.eligibleApproval passthrough at application.service.ts:236, never set by any UI); (2) isApprovalEligible is called only by getUpcomingStatus (UI labels, moderator/applications/page.tsx:49,1070) — never in approve(), approveUnauth(), approve-v2, or validate_application_for_approval; (3) submitFinal:177-189 and isUpcomingApplication:156-179 classify with startYear > new Date().getFullYear() (calendar year), while the locked rule uses the anchor-aware currentSessionStartYear (:229-234).
SCENARIO: in January-June a student selects session 2026-27 (startYear 2026 ≤ 2026) → typed fresh, eligibleApproval null, isUpcomingApplication false → the upcoming intercept at :342 is skipped → approval consumes/releases seats immediately while the outgoing 2025-26 students still hold them; with eligible_approval never populated, isApprovalEligible would return true even if it were called.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ Application ↔ expiry calendar. USER/DATA: premature seat allocation in the pre-anchor window (current students displaced / "capacity full" while seats are nominally held by next-session riders); the cron's eligibility reminder is commented out (cron/cleanup-expired-students:309-311).
REMED: call deriveCreationCategorisation at submission, enforce isApprovalEligible inside approve()/approve-v2 before claiming (and as a SQL predicate in validate_application_for_approval), and use the anchor-aware classifier everywhere.
EVIDENCE: zero callers of deriveCreationCategorisation (grep); application.service.ts:33,236,342-354; application-eligibility.ts:112-118,126,218-243.
APP-07 | P3 | HIGH | SPECIFICATION–IMPLEMENTATION CONTRADICTION — Unlocked and UI-hidden state transitions.
FILES: application.service.ts:257-304,342-354; cron/cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:349-358; repository.pg.ts:336-352.
INVARIANT: every state change goes through the claim/lease or transition_application_state.
CURRENT: no .tsx calls /api/applications/verify-upcoming (grep) yet that endpoint performs draft|submitted → verified_upcoming with no processing_lock, and repository.update ignores ROWCOUNT (success reported on a 0-row update); approve() releases the lease (:344) before verifyUpcoming (:346); the cron flips submitted → expired with no state predicate and no lease.
SCENARIO: moderator A claims and starts the capacity increment while moderator B calls verify-upcoming → state becomes verified_upcoming mid-approval while finalize_application_approval (state-blind, schema:910-916) deletes the row → a "verified upcoming" application silently consumed a seat, contradicting the documented rule (application.service.ts:247-256); or the cron's unguarded UPDATE expires an application that was just verified (it read the row earlier); or verify-upcoming returns success after the row was already deleted.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Application ↔ Session activation ↔ Seat. USER/DATA: lost applications, seats consumed by rows the UI shows as pending.
REMED: route these through WHERE state = ANY(...) + rowcount checks and a lease; remove or gate the UI-hidden endpoint; release the lease only after the state write.
EVIDENCE: application.service.ts:273-283,344-346; cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:354,358; no UI caller of verify-upcoming.
APP-08 | P3 | HIGH | SPECIFICATION–IMPLEMENTATION CONTRADICTION — Documented state machine unreachable; transition_application_state has no ownership or lock check.
FILES: schema:989-1025; api/applications/submit/route.ts; application.service.ts:166; lib/services/integrity-detector.ts:48.
INVARIANT: draft → awaiting_verification → verified → submitted (types/application.ts:9) is the only path to submitted.
CURRENT: no writer for awaiting_verification/verified (grep), so /api/applications/submit can only succeed on rows already in verified — never; save-draft/submit have no UI callers; transition_application_state selects the row with no FOR UPDATE and never compares p_actor_uid to v_app.applicant_uid (only writes the actor into state_history), while the route requires only a valid token; integrity-detector.ts:48 treats awaiting_verification/verified as live states, so those integrity checks are vacuous.
SCENARIO: any authenticated student posts {applicationId: "<someone else's id>"} → forced verified → submitted with the attacker recorded as actor; combined with submitted → rejected in the same map, any future route forwarding a client-supplied p_new_state would be a cross-tenant write.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Audit integrity ↔ Application. USER/DATA: forged actors; two orphaned endpoints that can never complete their documented flow.
REMED: verify applicant_uid = p_actor_uid and role inside the RPC, add FOR UPDATE; implement the missing states or delete save-draft/submit and the dead branches (including integrity-detector's LIVE set).
EVIDENCE: schema:996-1023; application.service.ts:166; integrity-detector.ts:48.
APP-09 | P3 | MEDIUM | SPECIFICATION–IMPLEMENTATION CONTRADICTION — Documented approval guard isApprovalEligible is never called by any approval path (see APP-06 for the full three-part break).
FILE: application-eligibility.ts:112-118,125-126.
CURRENT: comment asserts enforcement "in both approval routes via isApprovalEligible"; grep shows the only caller is getUpcomingStatus :144, used solely for moderator/admin UI labels. approve() imports only isUpcomingApplication (application.service.ts:33).
SCENARIO: an application with a client-set or legacy eligible_approval in the future displays "waiting_for_eligibility" in the moderator UI while Application.approve() will still accept it and consume a seat — UI and server disagree on the same invariant.
CROSS-DOMAIN: UI ↔ Application ↔ Seat. USER/DATA: moderators believe a date gate exists that the API does not enforce.
REMED: enforce in approve()/approve-v2 (and as a SQL predicate), or delete the claim from the docs.
EVIDENCE: application-eligibility.ts:112-118,126,140-144; application.service.ts:33; approve-v2/route.ts:71.
APP-10 | P4 | MEDIUM | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT — Draft rows shadow submitted applications (NULLS FIRST).
FILES: repository.pg.ts:220-229,185; renew-services/route.ts:156-168.
CURRENT: findByApplicantUid orders submitted_at DESC (Postgres default NULLS FIRST), so a draft (NULL submitted_at) sorts ahead of a submitted row; the single-row consumer then reports the draft's state, and submitFinal:175-181 uses the same first-row logic for its live-state dedupe.
SCENARIO: a stale draft (SSY NULL) masks a live submitted renewal → bulk renew proceeds and a second renewal request is admitted for a student already in flight; status APIs show "draft" while the UI shows submitted.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Application ↔ Payment dedupe. USER/DATA: duplicate renewal requests; confusing status.
REMED: order submitted_at DESC NULLS LAST (or filter drafts) and pick the newest live row.
EVIDENCE: repository.pg.ts:224-226; application.service.ts:175-181; renew-services/route.ts:156-168.
APP-11 | P4 | HIGH | TEST-HARNESS DEFECT — Application/capacity logic has only a delegation smoke test; the advertised compensation path is provably inert.
FILES: application/__tests__/application.service.test.ts:1-20; application.service.ts:536-543.
CURRENT: the suite mocks the repository and asserts getById passthrough; no coverage of approve()'s 3-step sequence, lease expiry, retry idempotency, capacity pairing, or the renewals path; __seatCompensation has exactly one occurrence repo-wide (:537, a read) so the documented "C4 compensating rollback" never runs; session-activation-concurrency.test.ts mocks incrementBusCapacity, so it cannot observe counter drift.
SCENARIO: a refactor of the approval sequence ships green while introducing double-increment (APP-03) — nothing catches it.
CROSS-DOMAIN: quality gate for Seat ↔ Application. USER/DATA: capacity bugs reach production undetected.
REMED: integration tests against real Postgres for approve/finalize/retry and reconcile; delete or implement __seatCompensation.
EVIDENCE: application.service.test.ts:14-19; grep: single hit at application.service.ts:537.
(3) UNVERIFIED (evidence-only — not promoted to findings)
- U1 — Deployed schema drift: no supabase/migrations/* exists (only supabase/COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql, 2751 lines); APP-01, CAP-05 and the index-based claims assume live DB matches it. Not runtime-checkable under read-only, no-DB access.
- U2 — current_members live column type: plain vs generated determines which half of CAP-05 is real (a pg_catalog query would settle it; if generated, every explicit assignment in schema:1122,1145 would already be failing).
- U3 — Plan bus ids: reassign_students_atomically writes bus_id = <plan value> (schema:2238) while reassign-students/route.ts:103-107 accepts id/busId/busNumber; if any caller passes a bus number, id-only reconcile (CAP-06) would zero that bus's counters. Not confirmed any caller does.
- U4 — Dead RPCs: activate_session_batch (schema:2040) and cleanup_abandoned_application_locks (:1037) have zero callers in src/; the "stale lease cleanup" the design relies on does not run (lease expiry still permits re-claim, which is what makes APP-03 reachable). Classified as dead code, not a bug.
- U5 — Soft-block compensation: the exception-path PERFORM bus_increment_capacity(...) before RAISE (schema:1644-1649) is a plpgsql savepoint no-op (the whole TX aborts). Not claimed as a double-increment.
- U6 — FOR UPDATE … OR … LIMIT 1 (schema:1109,1141): with OR, Postgres may lock a row that is then discarded by LIMIT; no concrete failure demonstrated.
- U7 — payment_ledger outside artifacts: absent from the repo schema; could only exist if created outside version control (its absence plus the ignored error is APP-02's premise).
- U8 — Draft shadowing (APP-10): depends on draft rows existing in production; no UI caller of save-draft was found (grep), so the state may be unpopulated.
- U9 — Live behavior: no tests, builds, or mutations were run; all claims are static code-path analysis with compensating controls noted inline.

ADTU ITMS — Agent H Audit: Student Lifecycle / Soft-Block / Hard-Block / Expiry
1. LIFECYCLE STATE & TRANSITION TABLE
