2. FINDINGS (severe-first)
LC-01 (P1) | CONFIDENCE High | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT / FINANCIAL-INTEGRITY ADJACENT
FILES: src/domains/student/services/student.service.ts:49-96 (applyPaymentValidity), src/lib/payment/payment.service.ts:554-561,596,656, src/lib/services/admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:69-75
PATH: /api/payments/approve → approveOfflinePayment → applyPaymentValidityToStudent → applyPaymentValidity → update(...{status:'active'})
INVARIANT: entitlement tracks seat ownership exactly; a seat-released student must reclaim capacity before being active again.
CURRENT: reactivation writes status='active', new valid_until/soft_block/hard_block but never bus_increment_capacity and never seat_released_at=null. The student becomes entitled (marker deliberately ignored, transport-entitlement.ts:23-27) while occupiesSeat() returns false because of the marker (admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:70) → the student rides on a seat the bus no longer counts.
SCENARIO: student soft-blocked July 1 (seat released) → pays offline → admin approves → active + entitled, load still decremented → next cron tail reconcile (fires whenever any block/delete occurred) cements the under-count → next approval over-allocates the bus → physically overbooked bus.
CONCURRENCY: payment approval racing the daily cleanup cron/reconcile.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ Payment ↔ Entitlement ↔ Audit (only seat_marker_inconsistent, medium, non-repairing: integrity-detector.ts:101-109).
EFFECT: silent capacity drift; students denied a seat they paid for; audit trail says "reclaim likely missed".
FIX: make reactivation one RPC (capacity reclaim + seat_released_at=NULL + status/date update) or refuse payment-approval reactivation when wasSeatReleased().
EVID: student.service.ts:87-94; admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:70; integrity-detector.ts:101-109.
LC-02 (P1) | CONFIDENCE High | CONSISTENCY DEFECT (spec–impl contradiction)
FILES: server/socket-router.ts:177-183,168-224; src/app/api/waiting-flag/create/route.ts:71-75; src/app/api/get-bus-data/route.ts:10-35
PATH: WS auth → presence{busId} → bus_id === claimedBusId only; no status, no softBlock.
INVARIANT (module header, transport-entitlement.ts:13-14): every transport decision must derive from getTransportEntitlement; "no status-alone checks".
CURRENT: HTTP is gated (trip-status, notify-driver, check-active-trip, bus-pass QR) but WS presence/subscription, waiting-flag create, and get-bus-data are not. bus_id is never cleared at soft block (only capacity changes), so a soft-blocked/expired student keeps live GPS streams, can raise waiting flags that notify drivers, and can read any bus by ID.
SCENARIO: student expires July 1; cron soft-blocks; HTTP 403s appear but the already-open WS keeps streaming bus_location_* all year; a new WS connect still authorizes on bus_id.
CONCURRENCY: WS session established pre-block; no re-evaluation on status change (role cache is role-only, 90 s TTL).
CROSS-DOMAIN: Entitlement ↔ Realtime ↔ Notification (driver pings from blocked students).
EFFECT: blocked students retain privileged live data + driver attention.
FIX: run getTransportEntitlement (or at least status==='active') in the WS presence handler and in waiting-flag/create; re-check on each subscribe.
EVID: socket-router.ts:183; waiting-flag/create/route.ts:72-75; transport-entitlement.ts:13-14.
LC-03 (P1) | CONFIDENCE Medium-High | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT (concurrency)
FILES: src/lib/cleanup-helpers.ts:57-100,190-201; supabase/COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1912-1965,1572-1651
PATH A: two concurrent deleteUserAndData(uid,'student') (admin API + cron hard-delete, or two admins) → both read userData (L60) with seatReleasedAt null → both call cascade → both fleetService.onStudentDeleted → two decrements.
PATH B: delete reads state before a concurrent soft_block_student_with_seat_release commits → soft block decrements + sets marker → delete then decrements using the stale userData → double seat release.
INVARIANT: bus capacity is decremented at most once per student.
CURRENT: delete_student_cascade_v1 returns success:true even when it deleted 0 rows (SQL:1952-1961) → the second caller cannot detect "already gone"; the capacity decision uses a pre-transaction read, not a row lock.
SCENARIO: cron hard-deletes while an operator deletes the same student in the admin UI → load drops 2 → bus turns away a paying student.
CONCURRENCY: overlapping delete paths; delete vs soft-block RPC.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ Identity ↔ Audit (both may emit audit events).
EFFECT: permanent under-count until an admin runs reconcile-bus-loads.
FIX: decide capacity inside the cascade RPC under FOR UPDATE (return seatReleased boolean) and only decrement when the RPC reports the row was actually deleted; reject double deletes.
EVID: cleanup-helpers.ts:89,190-196; COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1945-1961.
LC-04 (P1) | CONFIDENCE Medium-High | RELIABILITY / DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT
FILE: src/app/api/cron/cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:62-210
PATH: batch snapshot taken once (L62) → hard-delete branch (L130-210) uses studentData from that snapshot with no re-read, unlike the soft-block branch which re-reads (L227).
INVARIANT: never delete a student whose state changed after the decision was computed.
CURRENT: all three safety gates use stale data: validUntil (L143-147), sessionEndYear (L121), and the "active in last 30 days" gate (L150) — which is itself degraded because lastActiveAt/lastLoginAt are not in PG_STUDENT_COLUMNS (student.repository.pg.ts:94) and last_login_at lives on users, not student_profiles, so the gate silently falls back to updated_at (a write timestamp, not activity).
SCENARIO: operator renews/pays mid-run (window = whole batch, minutes at 1000 students) → snapshot still shows expired validUntil/old updated_at → student hard-deleted; the payment then lands on a missing student (applyPaymentValidity returns false, student.service.ts:58) → money taken, no service, no record of why.
CONCURRENCY: cron vs admin renewal / payment approval / login.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Identity ↔ Payment ↔ Audit (student_hard_deleted still emitted, asserting a false before state).
EFFECT: wrongful irreversible deletion of a paying student.
FIX: re-read the row immediately before delete and re-run all three gates on the fresh record (mirror the soft-block pattern); source activity from users.last_login_at.
EVID: cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:138-166 vs :227-241; student.repository.pg.ts:94.
LC-05 (P2) | CONFIDENCE High | SPECIFICATION–IMPLEMENTATION CONTRADICTION
FILES: src/lib/utils/deadline-computation.ts:188,548; src/lib/types/deadline-config.ts:10,91; src/lib/utils/renewal-utils.ts:206-214
PATH: computeBlockDatesFromValidUntil → lifecycle.hardDelete = Date.UTC(effectiveYear **+2**, startMonth, startDay) → stored hard_block.
INVARIANT: "Hard delete ALWAYS occurs in sessionEndYear + 1" (documented 3×) and the student-facing message promises hardDelete at referenceYear + 1.
CURRENT: actual stored hard_block = expiry year +2 (e.g. valid until 2026-06-30 → deleted 2028-07-01), while getBlockingMessage tells the student "renewed by <hardDelete> 2027 or your account will be permanently deactivated."
SCENARIO: student acts on the warning in 2027, then account survives (benign) — or ops believes the spec "+1" and schedules data-retention/comms around the wrong year; the reverse reading makes the message a false threat.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Entitlement UI ↔ Compliance/communications.
EFFECT: policy/date disagreement between spec, code and user-visible copy.
FIX: pick one rule; align deriveAcademicLifecycle, the type docs and getBlockingMessage.
EVID: deadline-computation.ts:188; deadline-config.ts:10; renewal-utils.ts:212.
LC-06 (P2) | CONFIDENCE High | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT (dead safety net)
FILES: src/domains/student/repositories/student.repository.pg.ts:54-94 (no lastRenewalDate); src/lib/utils/renewal-utils.ts:130-135,180-185,258-263,313-318; src/app/api/renew-services/route.ts:217
INVARIANT: a student who renewed after the block date must never be blocked/deleted.
CURRENT: lastRenewalDate has no PG column and no entry in STUDENT_FIELD_MAP → renew-services writes it and pgStudentToRow silently drops it; every read site (cleanup-expired-students:81-86,235, renewal-utils) therefore always sees undefined, so all four renewalDate > validDate → return false guards are unreachable. shouldHardDeleteFromStoredDates has no validUntil-future guard of its own and relies solely on this dead field.
SCENARIO: a renewal extends valid_until but a block/hard-block computation fails (the catch at student.service.ts:82-84 swallows it) → stale hard_block in the past + dead guard → premature block/delete.
COMPENSATING CONTROL: the cron's explicit validUntilDate > today → skip (route L143-147) and shouldBlockAccessFromStoredDates:250 (validDate > currentDate → false) cover most paths — which is why this is P2, not P1.
FIX: persist last_renewal_date (column + field map) or delete the guards and add an explicit validUntil guard to shouldHardDeleteFromStoredDates.
EVID: student.repository.pg.ts:54-94; renewal-utils.ts:313-318.
LC-07 (P2) | CONFIDENCE High | RELIABILITY DEFECT
FILES: src/lib/expiry-check.ts:86-102; supabase/COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1691-1695
INVARIANT: each session gets 3 reminders (R1/R2/final).
CURRENT: gates are count<1 / count<2 / count<3 but expiry_reminder_count is never reset (grep: only incremented, never zeroed) and is not scoped to a session/year. Year 1 → 3 reminders, year 2 → 2, year 3 → 1, year 4+ → 0. Also, the atomic claim commits the increment before pgInsertNotification (L112-149) — a notification-insert failure consumes that reminder permanently.
SCENARIO: long-tenured students stop receiving any renewal notice and are silently soft-blocked/deleted.
CROSS-DOMAIN: Expiry ↔ Notification ↔ Lifecycle.
FIX: reset the counter when valid_until advances (in applyPaymentValidity/renewal RPCs), or make the gate last_expiry_reminder_sent_at-per-session; insert the notification before claiming, or claim+insert in one transaction.
EVID: expiry-check.ts:92-98; COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1691-1695.
LC-08 (P2) | CONFIDENCE High | RELIABILITY / CONSISTENCY DEFECT
FILE: src/app/api/cron/cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:431-444 + session-activation.service.ts:122-129
INVARIANT: session activation may not start until the soft block for that session completed.
CURRENT: the soft_block_completed_<year> marker is upserted at the end of every daily run with no gate on results.errors.length or on whether any student actually needed blocking. A run that soft-blocked only half the cohort (rest throwing, L294-297) still writes the marker, so activateUpcomingSessionApplications proceeds, allocates the new session's seats while unreleased seats are still counted → same double-booking class as LC-01.
CONCURRENCY: marker write vs operator changes during the run (L62 snapshot).
CROSS-DOMAIN: Seat ↔ Session-activation ↔ Audit.
FIX: write the marker only when errors.length === 0 (and ideally only when the run observed now ≥ activationDate), or store per-student block progress.
EVID: cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:431-444 (no error gate); session-activation.service.ts:123-129.
LC-09 (P2) | CONFIDENCE High | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT (+ TEST-HARNESS DEFECT: destructive harness live in prod)
FILE: src/app/api/admin/simulate-deadlines/route.ts:112-225
PATH: POST {execute:true, dryRun:false} → soft-block via RPC (L134) or hard-delete via a second, non-canonical path: Cloudinary destroy → FCM delete → waiting_flags delete → decrementBusCapacity (before deletion) → Firebase delete → deleteStudent/deleteUser.
INVARIANT: one canonical deletion cascade, safety-gated, audited.
CURRENT vs cron: (a) no validUntil-future guard, (b) no 30-day recency guard, (c) no sessionEndYear guard — eligibility is purely isPastHardDelete (L79), so simulatedDate far in the future selects every student; (d) does not call delete_student_cascade_v1 → applications + notifications survive while the identity rows vanish (orphans); (e) emits no student_hard_deleted audit event; (f) capacity is decremented before the delete — a mid-flight crash leaves capacity decremented with the student alive.
CONCURRENCY: runs concurrently with the real cron (both can soft-block/delete the same student).
EFFECT: one admin click can irreversibly mass-delete the entire student body with weaker safety than the cron and no audit.
FIX: delete this execution branch in production (dry-run only), or funnel it through deleteUserAndData + the same three safety gates + mandatory audit.
EVID: simulate-deadlines/route.ts:79,112,175-225.
LC-10 (P2) | CONFIDENCE High | CONSISTENCY DEFECT
FILES: src/lib/services/admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:69-75 vs src/domains/student/repositories/student.repository.pg.ts:531-532
INVARIANT: one definition of "occupies a seat".
CURRENT: reconcile says seatReleasedAt → never occupies (marker wins over status); pgGetBusOccupancyStats says status==='active' → occupies regardless of marker. Same student, two counters, opposite answers. The cron tail reconcile rewrites morning/evening_load using predicate A while dashboards/analytics report predicate B.
SCENARIO: LC-01's active+marker student — reconcile drops the bus's load, occupancy stats still count them → admins "fix" the wrong number, or chase a phantom delta.
FIX: extract one shared occupiesSeat() used by reconcile, occupancy stats, getSeatOccupyingStudents, and deleteUserAndData's shouldDecrement.
EVID: admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:70-73; student.repository.pg.ts:531.
LC-11 (P2) | CONFIDENCE Medium (code certain, config-conditional) | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT
FILE: src/lib/utils/deadline-computation.ts:148-216,527-550
INVARIANT: soft_block/hard_block bracket valid_until.
CURRENT: deriveAcademicLifecycle places expiry at Date.UTC(effectiveYear, startMonth, startDay) - 1 day, which only equals valid_until when the session start month is after January. For a January session start, computeBlockDatesFromValidUntil(validUntil=2026-12-31) yields effectiveYear=2026 → soft_block = 2026-01-01 (364 days before valid_until) and hard_block = 2028-01-01 (one year early vs the +2-year intent). Entitlement (softBlock > now) then denies an active, fully-paid student for the whole session (past_soft_block), and the cron would hard-delete a year ahead of schedule.
SCENARIO: ops reconfigures academicSessionStart to Jan 1 (fully supported by config + ?? 6 defaults) → mass lockout of active paying students.
COMPENSATING CONTROL: shouldBlockAccessFromStoredDates:250 returns false while valid_until is future, and the cron's validUntilDate > today gate delays deletion — but entitlement has no such guard, so denial is immediate.
FIX: derive lifecycle year from the expiry/anchor invariant (or clamp softBlock > validUntil) and add a validUntil guard to entitlement.
EVID: deadline-computation.ts:157,182,188,535,544; transport-entitlement.ts:131-136.
LC-12 (P3) | CONFIDENCE High | SPECIFICATION–IMPLEMENTATION CONTRADICTION
FILE: src/app/api/students/[id]/status/route.ts:8-10,41-44
INVARIANT: status changes must go through the atomic RPC (seat + marker + dates together).
CURRENT: admin PATCH writes status alone. (i) soft_blocked → no seat release, no marker → seat occupied forever under flag-on semantics; (ii) active reactivation of a soft-blocked student → no capacity reclaim, no softBlock refresh → soft_block stays in the past → entitlement still denies past_soft_block, so the operator's "reactivate" silently does nothing for access; (iii) enum omits hard_blocked/pending_deletion (inconsistent with validation-schemas.ts:46), and allows expired/suspended/inactive, which the cron never scans again (route.ts:62) → those students are neither blocked nor deleted and their seats are never released.
EFFECT: three divergent admin tools produce untracked capacity + unreachable states.
FIX: route status changes through a single RPC that also moves capacity/seat_released_at/soft_block, and include cron-scanned states in the enum.
EVID: status/route.ts:9,41-44; cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:62.
LC-13 (P3) | CONFIDENCE High | RELIABILITY DEFECT
FILES: src/app/api/cron/*/route.ts; vercel.json:2-18; src/lib/security/cron-auth.ts:7-19
INVARIANT: no overlapping/duplicated cron work.
CURRENT: (a) no lock/mutex/advisory-lock anywhere — cleanup-expired-students and session-activation can overlap with themselves (Vercel retries, manual re-trigger from admin/cron-jobs page, cold-start retries); safety rests entirely on per-student idempotency (good for soft block: RPC 409; good for expiry reminders: increment_expiry_reminder_count day-gate). (b) vercel.json registers /api/cron/expiry-check?type=main twice (Mar 1, Apr 1) plus Jun 1 — but expiry-check.ts:35-37 only matches Mar 1 / Apr 1 / Jun 15 for a July session, so the Jun 1 entry is a permanent no-op wake-up. (c) cleanup-expired-students is a single-pass, per-student-try loop: restart safety is fine (each student independent) but a partial failure is only visible in an audit row; there is no resume marker. (d) verifyCronAuth is fail-closed (cron-auth.ts:12-15, constant-time, length-guarded) — verified good.
FIX: remove the Jun 1 cron; add a Postgres advisory-lock/lease at the top of the two state-mutating crons; gate the summary audit on errors.length===0.
EVID: vercel.json:10-14; cron-auth.ts:12-19; cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:36-41.
LC-14 (P3) | CONFIDENCE Medium-High | DATA INTEGRITY / FINANCIAL-EVIDENCE DEFECT
FILES: src/lib/services/session-activation.service.ts:307-342; src/lib/cleanup-helpers.ts:94; delete_student_cascade_v1 SQL:1941
INVARIANT: activation is atomic; approval evidence survives.
CURRENT: (a) activation is not atomic despite the header (L20-24 claims a single transaction): checkBusCapacity → incrementBusCapacity → createUser → createStudent → applicationRepo.remove are 5 round-trips with only manual compensation (L337) — a process crash between increment and create leaves a phantom seat; if applicationRepo.remove throws after the student is created, the outer handler returns failed with the capacity already incremented and the student already written, and the next run increments again (double count) because the app is still verified_upcoming. (b) applicationRepo.remove(appId) (L342) destroys the approval record; payments.application_id becomes an orphan (no FK). (c) identity_activate_student upsert sets status='active' (SQL:727) without clearing seat_released_at → re-creates LC-01's marker divergence on the new-application path.
SCENARIO: serverless cold-start mid-activation → phantom seat; or a late verified application after activation_<year> exists → cron silently never activates it (L132-138) though admin manual trigger bypasses markers.
FIX: fold activation into one RPC; keep approved applications (archive instead of delete); clear seat_released_at in identity_activate_student.
EVID: session-activation.service.ts:313-342,132-138; COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:708-738.
LC-15 (P3) | CONFIDENCE High | DATA INTEGRITY DEFECT (scope of erasure) / BUSINESS-POLICY DECISION
FILES: src/lib/cleanup-helpers.ts:92-100,145-174; COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1912-1965
INVARIANT: deletions must not destroy evidence needed to explain money.
CURRENT: hard delete removes applications (incl. the approved record), notifications, waiting_flags, fcm_tokens, users, student_profiles, plus Firestore profile_update_requests and the Cloudinary photo — all irreversible (no pending_deletion soft-delete stage exists despite the state being referenced everywhere). Preserved: payments, processed_payments, audit_events (no FK to the user) — good. But payments.application_id / receipt_id now point at rows that no longer exist, so a refund/fee query cannot reconstruct what was approved.
AUTHORIZATION: admin/moderator with students.canDelete, admin-only for /api/admin/delete-student; moderators blocked from deleting admins/drivers — verified adequate.
EFFECT: financial totals survive; approval provenance does not.
FIX: soft-delete/archive applications (state deleted) instead of removing them, or snapshot the application JSON into payments.metadata at approval.
EVID: COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1941,1945-1950; cleanup-helpers.ts:94.
3. UNVERIFIED / SELF-REJECTED
- "Soft block twice → double seat release" — rejected as a primary finding: the RPC takes FOR UPDATE and rejects non-active rows (SQL:1588-1604), and the cron additionally guards status==='active' (L216) plus a fresh re-read (L227-241). Double release only materialises through the delete-vs-soft-block window (LC-03) or via simulate-deadlines (LC-09).
- "Cron blocks a student who just renewed" (soft-block TOCTOU) — real but millisecond-wide: fresh read L227 → check L239 → RPC L250. A renewal landing inside that gap soft-blocks a paid student until an operator notices. Not promoted (no compensating auto-heal; renew-services would fix it only if re-run). Evidence: cleanup-expired-students/route.ts:227-257.
- "Deleted-state access by old client" — compensated: adminAuth.deleteUser + revokeRefreshTokens (cleanup-helpers.ts:135-136), role cache invalidated (L128-131), and resolveUserRole returns role:'' when users row is gone (role-cache.ts:114) → requiredRoles check fails closed (api-security.ts:363). Client localStorage entitlement cache is 5 min (auth-context.tsx:27) → cosmetic staleness only; all server gates re-read PG.
- "Expiry racing login" — no state race (expiry-check only sends notifications), but see LC-04: the activity guard is built on the wrong column, so an actively-reading student can be hard-deleted. last_login_at exists only on users (SQL:85).
- "Deletion removing financial evidence" — payments and audit survive (see LC-15); no FK from payments to applications/students (SQL:274-311) so no cascade could remove them. The loss is approval provenance, not amounts.
- Duplicate-Vercel-cron rejection / 0 0 1 6 * semantics — not verifiable from the repo (Vercel platform behaviour).
- blockDatesComputedAt written at cleanup-expired-students:101 is silently dropped — no entry in STUDENT_FIELD_MAP; harmless (soft/hard block still persist) but the migration timestamp is never stored. Minor, not counted.
- Local vs UTC — deriveAcademicLifecycle and expiry-check are consistently UTC (good); daysBetween/normalizeLeapYearDate use local components (deadline-computation.ts:310-315,28) and simulate-deadlines uses simDate.getFullYear() local (L37,59) — ±1-day drift risk on the grace-period path; not elevated because the cron's primary gates are UTC/ISO-based.
▣  Explore · MiMo-V2.6-Flash Free · 19m 23s











ADTU ITMS — Agents I+J Audit: Driver/Bus Operations + Trip Lifecycle
1) TRIP LIFECYCLE + LOCK AUTHORITY SUMMARY
Lock authority = PostgreSQL only. There is no Redis trip lock. Uniqueness comes from active_trips table constraints bus_id UNIQUE, driver_id UNIQUE, trip_id UNIQUE + CHECK(status='active') (supabase/COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:338-345), enforced through acquire_trip_lock (INSERT → unique_violation handler) and end_trip_atomically (SELECT … FOR UPDATE). App-level preflight (tripStartPreflight) and canOperate are advisory only; Redis is used solely for the GPS jump/replay guard (gps-redis-guard.ts), which fails closed in prod (:488-491).
Authorization model = DYNAMIC, not static. Static bindings (buses.driver_uid, driver_profiles.bus_id/route_id) were deliberately dropped (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1281-1286); driver/check-active-trip:23-24 states "any driver can start any bus". UI and runtime agree: available-buses lists every non-inactive bus with is_operated_by_other, trip/start/page.tsx:67-78 and resolve-bus-qr apply the same two gates (other-driver lock, status==='inactive') that the server applies (trip-validation.service.ts:29,39-47). A driver can start any bus by ID — that is intended. What the DB does enforce: a driver cannot hold two trips (driver_id UNIQUE) and two drivers cannot hold one bus (bus_id UNIQUE) — proven by e2e/driver-authorization.spec.ts:101-106.
Lifecycle: START = preflight → acquire_trip_lock (self-heals rows with last_heartbeat < now-600s) → WS broadcast → FCM start (5 s bound). LIVENESS = /api/driver/heartbeat (60 s from UI, live-tracking/page.tsx:891) or GPS update writing last_heartbeat+expires_at=+600s (throttled 45 s). END = ownership check → end_trip_atomically (deletes lock + bus_locations, writes driver_trip_history, discards trips <600 s) → app-level cleanupTrip (waiting flags, device sessions) → broadcast → FCM end. CRON = cleanup_stale_locks(600s) writes completed_stale history + deletes lock. Both end-trip and end-journey-v2 are live and call the same endTrip(); end-trip is marked deprecated (Sunset 2026-09-01, already past) and the web UI uses end-journey-v2 (live-tracking:1352); duplication causes no double side effects because alreadyEnded short-circuits (trip-orchestrator.ts:238-241).
2) FINDINGS (severe-first)

3) UNVERIFIED / SELF-REJECTED CLAIMS
- Live-DB column drift (DRV-05, WAIT-01): COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql is the only schema artifact and is internally inconsistent (RPCs at :1163,1255-1259 reference fcm_start_sent/fcm_end_sent not present in the CREATE TABLE at :338-351). If the production table has extra columns added out-of-band, severity drops to "fragile code"; if not, those routes 500 today. Not confirmed against the live database.
- Cron cadence (TRIP-02): Vercel cron is the only schedule file found, but an external scheduler (docs/monitoring) could call /api/cron/cleanup-stale-locks more often; the route is POST-aliasable.
- TRIP-01 production impact: inferred from code order; no telemetry/logs were read to confirm riders actually never receive trip-end pushes.
- Redis availability in prod (TRIP-09): the fail-closed branch requires NODE_ENV=production && REDIS_URL && !ALLOW_INSECURE_LOCAL_GPS_FALLBACK; env values were not read.
- Mobile client deviceId (DRV-04): no mobile client source in repo — unknown whether it sends x-device-id/deviceId (determines whether (d) is a real false-rejection).
- Rejected as weak: "driver can start another bus by ID" (by design — dynamic binding, RULES.md:132, schema:1281-1286); "driver can start two buses concurrently"/"two drivers one bus" (both hard-blocked by driver_id/bus_id UNIQUE, schema:341-342); "END×END duplicates history" (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING + alreadyEnded); "END racing cleanup corrupts history" (both writers use ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING; FOR UPDATE serializes end_trip_atomically); "Redis unavailable breaks trip uniqueness" (uniqueness is DB-only).
- Compensating controls confirmed present: active_trips three-way unique constraints; end_trip_atomically atomic lock+history+bus_locations in one transaction; bus FOR UPDATE in acquire_trip_lock closing the deactivation TOCTOU (:1189); GPS strictly gated by checkActiveTrip (gps-persistence.service.ts:25-63) so no cross-driver location writes; two partial unique indexes on waiting_flags (schema:596-597); trip-lock verification in ack-flag/mark-boarded/respond-wait/waiting-flag/acknowledge; rate limits + role gates on all driver routes; QR = AES-256-GCM + 128-bit HMAC + expiry (encryption.service.ts:176-241); test coverage at end-trip-atomicity.test.ts, cleanup-trip-ordering.test.ts, trip-concurrency-multidriver.test.ts, ack-flag-auth.test.ts, device-session.test.ts.
- Noted but below threshold: mark-boarded accepts raised→boarded though RULES.md:288 requires acknowledged|waiting, and tests for a non-existent picked_up status (mark-boarded:42,58 vs schema:380); verifyDriverBusAssignment name implies an assignment check but performs none (trip-validation.service.ts:59-62); check_bus_lock/get_stale_locks/release_trip_lock RPCs are never called by app code (dead spec surface, RULES.md:178,515); cleanup-stale-locks deletes only raised/acknowledged flags, never waiting (route.ts:87-90); respond-wait does not verify studentId belongs to busId (respond-wait:31).

DynamicStudentMap has no such guard DynamicStudentMap.tsx:146-158.
Rate limits / ordering / failure summary: 240 req/min/driver/path (per-container unless Upstash rate-limiter.ts:61-62,121-162); ordering = Lua HSET FIFO (single socket) → 1 packet global lag only if RESP desync (GPS-01); duplicates accepted & re-broadcast (no dedup idempotency key); client never retries → drops are silent; DB write failure after WS publish leaves live map OK + DB fallback stale; WS publish failure returns 200 anyway.
WORKLOAD MODEL (constants from code: 2 s cadence = 0.5 Hz; active-trip SELECT 1/req; device-session SELECT 1/8 s/driver; Redis EVAL 1/req; PUBLISH 1/req; breadcrumb 1/60 s; heartbeat 1/45 s; student poll 1/25 s healthy, 1/5 s degraded; WS frame 1/req × N watchers; msg ≈200 B)
Buses	HTTP req/s	DB q/min (ingest)	Redis ops/min (EVAL+PUBLISH)	WS frames/min (local)	WS delivered/s @N=20	trip-status polls/s @N=20
1	0.5	~40	60	30	10	0.8
10	5	~400	600	300	100	8
25	12.5	~1 000	1 500	750	250	20
50	25	~2 000 (33 q/s)	3 000 (50/s)	1 500 (25/s)	500 msg/s ≈100 KB/s	40 req/s (WS up) / 200 req/s (WS down)
Notes: student fallback polling (40 req/s @50×20) exceeds GPS ingest (25 req/s) and each poll costs ≥1 active_trips read. With K Next containers, breadcrumb/heartbeat writes scale to K/min/bus (throttle maps are process-local). Lua ships full script each EVAL (700 B @25/s ≈ 17 KB/s) — no EVALSHA.
Storage growth/day: bus_locations = 1 row/bus (UNIQUE bus_id COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:355, upsert route.ts:116-127) → ≤50 rows ≈15 KB + 4 btree indexes; growth 0/day; deleted at trip end (end_trip_atomically:1422, trip-orchestrator.ts:248); no partitioning, no retention cron for location tables (only cleanup_old_trip_history 90 d :1441-1451). driver_location_updates (:407-418) has no INSERT anywhere in src/ or server/, no index beyond PK, no cleanup → 0 rows, 0 growth (README claims it is the breadcrumb store supabase/README.md:24). Redis gps:last:* = B keys ×70 B, TTL 20 min.

3) UNVERIFIED / COMPENSATING CONTROLS
- No runtime evidence: all figures derive from code constants (2 s cadence, 45 s/60 s throttles, 240 req/min, TTL 1200 s, 600 s trip lock, 25 s/5 s student poll). No logs, metrics, DB rows, Redis keys or live traffic were inspected; container count K, REDIS_URL password/TLS usage, WS_PRIVILEGED_TOKEN presence and fleet size are unknown — GPS-01/02/03/07/11 severity is conditional on them.
- Redis TLS unsupported in gps-redis-guard.ts:67-79,164 (scheme ignored, plain net.createConnection; same pattern in server/redis-client.ts:59, role-cache-redis.ts:146) — a rediss:// endpoint would never connect ⇒ GPS-07 fail-closed. UNVERIFIED whether the deployment uses TLS.
- Compensating controls found: single-device conflict is also gated client-side (checkDeviceSession fail-closed session-device-service.ts:108-137); trip lock survives Redis outages via the independent 60 s /api/driver/heartbeat; student marker staleness is bounded by HTTP fallback polls (25 s/5 s) with a monotonic guard (track-bus/page.tsx:541-603), an ended-trip tombstone (location-packet-guard.ts:69-71,85-111), trip_ended cache clearing (socket-router.ts:300-301, redis-broadcast.ts:113-115) and 60 s snapshot expiry; DB footprint is bounded by UNIQUE(bus_id) + deletes at trip end; transport queues GPS coalesced per channel rather than dropping blindly; rate limiting can be Upstash-distributed when configured.
- Not audited: end-to-end TLS/proxy behaviour, Supabase pool sizing under the 33 q/s ingest figure, server/offline-queue.ts, RLS runtime effect (GPS writes go through service_role), and actual watcher counts.

(4) UNVERIFIED / SELF-REJECTED
- Not executed here (read-only): e2e/cross-node-redis.spec.ts, e2e/bus-isolation.spec.ts, scripts/staging/master-simulation.ts (“Cross-node VERIFIED”) — these are the only artifacts that would prove ws1→Redis→ws2 fan-out and per-bus isolation end-to-end; unit coverage is 2 files (server/websocket-server.test.ts, server/privileged-guard.test.ts) with redis-broadcast and authenticateSocket mocked, so no test covers WS-01/WS-02/REDIS-01/REDIS-02 — the untested areas are exactly where the confirmed findings sit. Revocation is proven at handler level (privileged-guard.test.ts:135-245) but only when initRedisBroadcastRelay is invoked directly.
- .env contains WS_HOST/WS_SERVER_URL (names only inspected, values not read): therefore the claim “docker-compose.prod.yml lacks a WS bridge URL” is downgraded to UNVERIFIED — the value is almost certainly supplied by .env. If it points only at ws1, all event publication has a single node SPOF (ws2 never publishes: no local broadcast origin exists since location_update is a no-op, socket-router.ts:272-279) — plausible but unconfirmed.
- Offline queue behaviour (offline-queue.ts:30-50): frames shed to enqueueOffline for a socket that is open but saturated are only delivered on that uid’s next connection — the current socket never receives them. Bounded (500/msg, 5 min TTL, drop-oldest 10 %) and intentionally documented (websocket-server.ts:24-27); classified EXPECTED, not a finding.
- Dead code (OPTIMIZATION OPPORTUNITY): broadcastToChannels (websocket-server.ts:366-405) has zero callers; socket-middleware.ts use() is never called so the middleware chain is always transparent; pollRedisStats opens a fresh raw TCP connection every 5 s even when REDIS_URL is unset (defaults to redis://redis:6379) — noise only.
- liveBusLocations (socket-router.ts:71-90): entries are cleared on trip_ended (local + via relay redis-broadcast.ts:113-115) and lazily after 60 s on read — stale-snapshot risk bounded at 60 s; client applies a monotonic timestamp guard (track-bus/page.tsx:483-489, :587-593), so an old subscribe-time snapshot cannot overwrite newer client state except when state was nulled by trip_ended — self-rejected as a defect.
- Presence/channel auth for admin (socket-router.ts:201-203, unconditional authorized = true) is a deliberate policy (admins are then blocked from waiting flags at :100-105) — SPECIFICATION decision, not a bug.
- Dev-only privileged-token mismatch (transport/websocket.ts:6 sends '__server__' while authenticator.ts:15-20 mints an ephemeral token when unset): local bridge silently disabled; harmless, production is gated by assertPrivilegedTokenSafe (index.ts:21-28).
- Out of scope but observed: nginx/certs/privkey.pem is committed to the repo; server/Dockerfile:45 runs npx tsx (interpreted TS) in the production image.

CODE PATH: :102-116 lock (acquire_fcm_lock) consumed before sending → :149-170 multicast → errors caught at :175-177 → :186-199 metrics
INVARIANT: metrics reflect actual delivery; a failed send can be retried.
CURRENT: if FCM throws after the lock, the trip event is permanently unsendable (NOTIFICATION_ALREADY_SENT on retry) yet the call returns success:true; directTokensCount = tokens.length (:172) counts failed tokens as sent, failureCount forced to 0 when any success (:194); acquire_fcm_lock returns acquired:true for non-UUID trip_id (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1248-1252) → idempotency silently disabled for legacy ids. NOTIF-04 | P3 | MEDIUM | CONSISTENCY DEFECT
AFFECTED: C:\Users\ADMIN\Desktop\Projects\ITMS\src\lib\services\fcm-notification-service.ts, C:\Users\ADMIN\Desktop\Projects\ITMS\src\app\api\save-fcm-token\route.ts
CODE PATH: fcm-notification-service.ts:136-184 (direct multicast chosen iff tokens.length>0; topic fallback only when 0) + save-fcm-token\route.ts:66-87 (subscribe route_*, bus_* topics) — no unsubscribe path anywhere
INVARIANT: a device receives a trip event exactly once, and stops receiving topics it no longer belongs to.
CURRENT: (a) if ≥1 valid token exists the topic path is skipped → topic-subscribed devices without a stored token get nothing; if 0 tokens exist the topic path fires → devices with stale-but-valid:true tokens get a duplicate whenever they also re-subscribe; (b) students never unsubscribe from old route_*/bus_* topics on bus reassignment → stale-topic pushes continue. DB-then-FCM ordering in notifications\create\route.ts:365-373 is correct (row committed first, push best-effort → in-app row survives FCM failure), but a retried POST after 15 s creates a second row + second push (dedup window only 15 s, rate limit 10/min mitigates). CONCURRENCY: bus change + trip start. CROSS-DOMAIN: GPS/trip notifications. REMEDIATION: single dispatch strategy per event (tokens XOR topic), unsubscribe on assignment change, idempotency key for broadcast sends. EVIDENCE: src\lib\services\fcm-notification-service.ts:118-134,180-184; src\app\api\save-fcm-token\route.ts:66-87; src\app\api\notifications\create\route.ts:365-373.
(3) UNVERIFIED / COMPENSATING CONTROLS
1. Supabase max-rows cap (MEDIUM confidence): no .limit() anywhere in the cron read path; the 1000-row default is a PostgREST/platform setting not visible in-repo. Compensating: daily re-runs and absolute-count reconciliation partially mask truncation; pgFindNotificationsByUser explicitly limits to 50.
2. Vercel cron semantics (UNVERIFIED in-repo): whether Vercel skips an invocation while the previous run is in flight, retries failed invocations, or rejects the three duplicate path entries for expiry-check?type=main (vercel.json:3-15). Compensating: all jobs are written to be re-runnable; only CRON-08 (notify-then-mark) and CRON-02 (stale snapshot) break under overlap.
3. Function duration: no export const maxDuration on any cron route → platform default applies (exact value depends on plan/fluid compute, not in-repo). Compensating: cleanup-expired-students isolates per-item errors, but the tail marker/audit are lost on timeout (CRON-04).
4. Schema drift (NOTIF-02): production DB may have been altered outside the repo (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS + no migration folder for D9/D10); cannot confirm without DB access.
5. CRON_SECRET delivery: assumes Vercel injects Authorization: Bearer <CRON_SECRET> automatically; .env/.env.docker hold a real secret but are gitignored (verified not tracked) and Dockerfile:67 sets CRON_SECRET=ci only in the builder stage (not inherited by the runner stage) → a Docker deployment without .env fails closed (401) rather than running unauthenticated.
6. RULES.md:892 documents an x-cron-secret header that cron-auth.ts does not accept (Bearer only) — spec/impl mismatch, fail-closed so no security impact. Not counted as a finding.
7. verifyCronAuth compares UTF-16 length before timingSafeEqual (cron-auth.ts:18-19): a same-length multi-byte token throws RangeError → surfaces as 500 (still fail-closed).
8. Notifications are never a source of truth (checked): idempotency state lives on student_profiles.expiry_reminder_count, applications.eligible_reminder_sent_at, applications.processing_lock, markers, and active_trips.fcm_*_sent — the notifications table is only read for display (api\notifications\route.ts:24). No finding here.
9. In-process server/ schedulers (heartbeat-service.ts:16, message-validator.ts:11, offline-queue.ts:14, rate-limiter.ts:49) are memory-only, unref()'d, single-process, no DB/Redis side effects, torn down via stop*() — no overlap or restart-safety issue. scripts/ contains no scheduler.

10. src\app\admin|moderator mutation flows beyond cache invalidation calls (read only the invalidateCollectionCache call sites).
▣  Explore · MiMo-V2.6-Flash Free · 11m 28s



ITMS Performance Audit — Agents S+T Final Deliverable
Scope: Next.js runtime performance + database hot paths. Read-only. Scale basis: 50 buses, 10,000 students, burst = 10,000 students on the app within minutes.

2. PERFORMANCE / RESOURCE DEFECT FINDINGS
F-1 (P0) — Serial request pipeline multiplies burst latency. Every API request runs, strictly in sequence: verifyIdToken → resolveUserRole (2 PG queries on a 2,000-entry cache) → applyRateLimit (blocking Upstash REST POST) → handler queries (api-security.ts:339,340,378; rate-limiter.ts:134). Nothing is done in parallel, and rate limiting happens after auth (api-security.ts:372-392), so abusive traffic still pays full token + role cost before being rejected. Under a 10k burst this is the first bottleneck: per-request overhead is dominated by non-handler work.
F-2 (P0) — Notification read-state is an O(N²) write amplification. A single admin broadcast writes a ~10k-element recipient_ids array into one row (create/route.ts:345-351, notificationToPgRow at notification.repository.pg.ts:200). Every subsequent read of that row pulls all four ID arrays and edit_history (PG_NOTIFICATION_COLUMNS, line 88), and every "mark as read" does a read-modify-write of a 10k-element read_by_user_ids (notification.service.ts:206-223) — racy, and the array doubles the row on every broadcast cycle. Marking one notification read costs 3 full row reads + 1 student read + 1 write: findById for visibility ([id]/read/route.ts:31), getStudentByUid (:41), then markAsRead's own findById (:210) before the update.
F-3 (P0) — Broadcast send is synchronous and serial inside the request. Despite the // non-blocking comment (create/route.ts:367), FCM send is awaited: 50 sequential token queries of 200 IDs each (fcm-token.repository.pg.ts:97-116) followed by up to 20 sequential sendEachForMulticast calls (create/route.ts:244-258). At 10k recipients this will exceed any reasonable function timeout and leave an already-inserted notification only partially pushed.
F-4 (P1) — Admin dashboard does 6 unbounded full-table scans of payments. dashboard-counts selects the whole ledger to compute 4 numbers (route.ts:58), and the two payment/analytics calls each re-run getPaymentStats and getPaymentMethodTrend (payments-supabase.ts:942-944,997-1000) for days and months modes. All aggregation happens in JS over full row sets (:951-974), and getPaymentTrend is O(7×N). This is the single largest and fastest-growing read cost in the codebase.
F-5 (P1) — audit_events has zero indexes. The only occurrences in the schema are the CREATE TABLE (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:422), RLS (:2482) and a GRANT (:2652). pgQueryAuditEvents does count: exact, an ILIKE %term% OR over four columns, and ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT/OFFSET (audit.repository.pg.ts:141,158-170) — every admin page view is a full scan + full sort, twice. pgInsertAuditEvent also does .select('id') on insert (:121), an extra round trip that serves no caller.
F-6 (P1) — Two independent identity read stacks double every page load. resolveUserRole already called getUserById inside withSecurity, then /api/auth/user calls it again (auth/user/route.ts:24) — 4 profile queries per page load — and fires an unconditional updateUser({lastLoginAt}) write (:35) that is pure write amplification at burst scale.
F-7 (P1) — Redundant profile reads on the student 30-second poll. requireTransportEntitlement loads student_profiles (require-transport-entitlement.ts:31) and getStudentProfileAndShift loads the identical row again (student-shift-resolver.ts:17) in the same handler, contradicting the inline comment at trip-status/route.ts:37-39.
F-8 (P1) — Client retry policy amplifies overload. authApiFetch retries 429 and 5xx up to 2 times with backoff and a 12 s timeout (secure-api-client.ts:15,59-79). Combined with F-1, a burst that produces 429s will generate up to 3× the intended request volume.
F-9 (P1) — Realtime singleton is dead code; connections churn per navigation. src/hooks/useWebSocket.ts has zero call sites. Six separate components each construct and tear down their own WebSocketClient, so every page navigation re-handshakes and re-authenticates (server/authenticator.ts:92,101 = JWT verify + one PG query per handshake). A 10k burst lands 10k+ concurrent handshakes on a single-process http.createServer WS server (server/index.ts:39,81).
F-10 (P1) — Universal shell pulls role-specific chrome into every bundle. AppShell statically imports both AdminSidebar and ModeratorSidebar plus MapRuntimeBootstrap (which pulls pmtiles) for all routes, public ones included (AppShell.tsx:3-20, wired at layout.tsx:90).
F-11 (P2) — Offset pagination with exact counts. useApiCollection sends limit/offset and reads X-Total-Count on every page (useApiCollection.ts:186,232-239,295-326); GET /api/students recomputes count: exact per page and does ORDER BY full_name with no supporting index (students/route.ts:89-105). Its auto-refresh effect depends on an unmemoised refresh (:372), so the interval is torn down and recreated on every render — churn, and refresh effectively rarely fires.
F-12 (P2) — Export endpoints build the entire payload in memory as JSON. payment/export returns every row of an academic year as one JSON object (payment/export/route.ts:34-84), and export-firestore loads 11 whole collections and JSON.stringifys them (export-firestore/route.ts:52-80). Both are unbounded and rate-limited only at 100/min (RateLimits.READ).
Positive findings (no change needed)
- No server-side request waterfalls. Zero route handlers call internal localhost HTTP APIs; the pattern is shared service modules + Promise.all. Verified by grep across src/app/api.
- Parallel query fan-out is used well on hot endpoints: dashboard-counts (10-way Promise.all, route.ts:50-61), student/dashboard-data (2 + 5, :27-36,107-113), fleet/live-status (5-way, :58-76).
- No N+1 in payment enrichment — enrichPaymentsWithStudentProfiles batches profile lookups into one query (payments-supabase.ts:624-670); enrichBusListWithDriverUid batches into one (fleet.repository.pg.ts:150-163).
- GPS ingest is properly throttled: device-session cache, MIN_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS = 45 s, MIN_BREADCRUMB_INTERVAL_MS = 60 s (location-write-throttle.ts:1,27), Redis atomic guard with FIFO queue, 2 s negative lock cache (gps-persistence.service.ts:25-40).
- Heavy/exceljs is dynamically imported (export-helpers.ts:14-18), jsPDF dynamically imported, map components largely next/dynamic, and the admin dashboard splits 9 chart widgets into dynamic chunks (admin/page.tsx:18-50).
- Cron schedules are spread out (10 crons across distinct hours — vercel.json), no thundering-herd overlap.
- Singleton Supabase clients with a 10 s AbortSignal.timeout (supabase-server.ts) prevent client-construction storms.
3. UNVERIFIED (evidence only — no index/optimisation recommendation without measurement)
These are observed facts I could not quantify without a running database or profiler. No read-benefit vs write-cost conclusion is asserted.
 1. Index gaps on hot filters. student_profiles has no index on full_name (used by ORDER BY full_name at students/route.ts:91,104) or a composite (status, full_name). payments has status, created_at, student_uid, payment_id, Razorpay IDs but no index on transaction_date, which is the range predicate + sort key for getPaymentsForExport, getCompletedPaymentsForReporting and getPaymentTrend* (payments-supabase.ts:785-793,903-906,1037-1038). payments also has no index on method. Current payments row count and the selectivity of these predicates were not measurable here — read benefit unknown; write cost is +1 b-tree maintenance per insert/update on a write-heavy table. Requires EXPLAIN (ANALYZE) on real data before any recommendation.
 2. ILIKE %term% search over full_name,email,enrollment_id with OR (students/route.ts:87-92) and over four audit_events columns (audit.repository.pg.ts:158-163) cannot use a B-tree index regardless. A pg_trgm GIN index would have real write cost on student_profiles (10k rows, updated on profile edits) — not evaluated without measured query frequency.
 3. bus_locations unbounded select in fleet/live-status (route.ts:72) returns every row (≈50 at 50 buses — likely fine, but a LIMIT is absent so growth is unbounded if rows aren't upserted per bus).
 4. routes.stops payload shipped on every 8 s fleet poll and in getAllBuses/routeService.getAll — actual byte size not measured.
 5. Role cache sizing: ROLE_CACHE_MAX = 2000 vs 10,000 students implies thrash, but the per-instance Node memory headroom (Render/Vercel instance size) was not observable, so a concrete MAX value is not recommended.
 6. WS server capacity: server/index.ts exposes no maxPayload/connection-limit/compression tuning; single-process createServer with a 90 s token auth cache capped at 1,000 entries (authenticator.ts:55-59,112). Connection memory per socket and available RSS are unknown — no concurrency limit can be stated.
 7. Bundle sizes: page TSX file sizes were observed (student/page.tsx ≈86 KB source, admin/applications ≈87 KB, (landing)/page.tsx ≈75 KB, (auth)/apply/form ≈71 KB) but no production next build was run, so actual First Load JS / gzip numbers are unavailable. next.config.ts sets splitChunks with minSize: 20000 and named firebase/supabase/ui-libs groups (next.config.ts:111-141) — effectiveness unverified.
 8. Firebase client SDK weight: src/lib/firebase.ts:10-12 eagerly imports firebase/firestore, firebase/storage and firebase/messaging and initializes all three on every page (:49-64), while only auth is imported by auth-context.tsx:3. Whether tree-shaking removes the unused services under Turbopack is unverified (they are not in optimizePackageImports as a group in a way that guarantees this).
 9. Schema/code drift on audit_events. The repository reads/writes action, category, severity, summary, actor_name, target_*, metadata (audit.repository.pg.ts:104-116,139-170) while COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:422-432 defines event_type, actor_email, actor_role, ip_address, user_agent, details. Either a later migration exists outside supabase/*.sql (only one .sql file is present) or these calls fail against a fresh database. Correctness, not performance — flagged for the owning agent; no perf conclusion drawn.
10. /api/applications/all and /api/students bypass withSecurity but do call verifyApiAuth/resolveUserRole directly — their interaction with the O-01 Upstash limiter differs from withSecurity routes and was not traced end-to-end for total auth cost.
11. Impact of next.config.ts header rules: five headers() rules are evaluated per request with a negative-lookahead regex on HTML routes (next.config.ts:279) — cost per request unmeasured.
12. suppressConsoleWarnings() at module scope of the root layout (layout.tsx:27) — mutates global console behaviour; performance implication unmeasured.
▣  Explore · MiMo-V2.6-Flash Free · 12m 37s



ADTU ITMS — INFRASTRUCTURE + OBSERVABILITY/SRE AUDIT (read-only)
Scope covered: Dockerfiles, both compose files, nginx+certs, prometheus/alertmanager/grafana, terraform (skim), vercel.json, env files (names only), next.config.ts, scripts/{validate-env,validate-config,preflight,health-check,diagnose,incident-bundle,maintenance-mode,deploy-compose,wait-healthy}, observability lib, api/{metrics,health,sre,tracing}, server metrics/logging, sensitive-logging greps.

Counts: prevented YES = 9 · NO = 15 · UNVERIFIED = 1 · Detailed below = 12 (P2×3, P3×9). Matrix-only YES-defects: #8 (P4), #18 (UNVERIFIED/P4).
2. DETAILED WRITE-UPS (12)
#C — Captured renewal payment with no application (FINANCIAL INTEGRITY, P2, HIGH)
processCapturedPayment writes the payment row, then calls submitFinal(...) and discards its return value (src/lib/payment/payment.service.ts:382, self-heal path :182). submitFinal returns {success:false, error:'An application is already in progress'} — it does not throw — whenever findByApplicantUid(uid) finds an app in a LIVE_STATES and existing.state === 'submitted' (src/domains/application/services/application.service.ts:170-175). The function still returns {status:'success'} (payment.service.ts:439), the webhook replies HTTP 200 (src/app/api/payment/webhook/razorpay/route.ts:101), so Razorpay performs no retry. Preconditions: student has a submitted fresh application and pays online for a renewal. Worst case: money captured, no renewal request exists, no error path, no compensation, only manual reconciliation of payments vs applications. Fix: check submitFinal result; return 500 so Razorpay retries.
#A — getClientIp trusts client headers (SECURITY, P2, MEDIUM)
getClientIp returns x-real-ip, else cf-connecting-ip, else the leftmost x-forwarded-for entry, with no trusted-proxy allowlist (src/lib/security/api-security.ts:120-133). That IP forms the skipAuth rate-limit key ip:${ip}:${limiterKey} (:374-378) — i.e. the only throttle on unauthenticated endpoints. On any deployment where the edge does not overwrite these headers, an attacker rotates them to obtain unlimited fresh counters → credential stuffing / OTP / GPS flooding. Fix: take the right-most untrusted-hop-verified entry or a platform-fixed header, never the leftmost.
#15 — Retry/idempotency gap on renewal submission (FINANCIAL INTEGRITY, P2, HIGH)
Same root cause as C but reached via any retry: payment verify, webhook delivery, or /payment/recover. Everything else in the retry family is genuinely idempotent — submit-final uses deterministic applicationId = body.applicationId || uid plus a LIVE_STATES guard (application.service.ts:165-175,215,239); approval is CAS-leased (SQL:844-851); waiting-flag insert handles 23505 (src/app/api/student/waiting-flag/route.ts:101). The single non-idempotent link is the ignored result.
#9 — acquire_trip_lock early-return ignores expires_at; cron is daily (RELIABILITY, P3, HIGH)
acquire_trip_lock first does SELECT trip_id … WHERE bus_id=… AND driver_id=… AND status='active' and returns alreadyActive:true (supabase/COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1182-1184) without expires_at > NOW() — and this return fires before the stale-row DELETE at :1193-1195. TripLockService.startTrip calls the RPC directly with no expiry pre-check (src/lib/services/trip-lock-service.ts:142-181). Meanwhile vercel.json:28-31 schedules cleanup-stale-locks 0 4 * * * while the route's own comment claims "every minute" (src/app/api/cron/cleanup-stale-locks/route.ts:4-5,27). Net: after a 10-minute heartbeat lapse the driver tapping "Start Trip" gets the old trip id and old start_time; the zombie status='active' row can persist up to 24 h, skewing driver_trip_history durations and keeping stale waiting_flags alive. Mitigating reads that do honour expires_at: student/trip-status, driver/check-active-trip, driver/available-buses, fleet/live-status.
#11 — GPS fail-closed starves the GPS-path heartbeat (RELIABILITY, P3, HIGH)
Production is intentionally fail-closed (src/domains/gps/services/gps-redis-guard.ts:484-491 returns redis_unavailable), processUpdate returns accepted:false (gps-pipeline.service.ts:191-212), and POST /api/driver/update-location therefore returns 400 at :72-74 — before the throttled active_trips.last_heartbeat/expires_at write at :87-99. During any Redis outage no GPS breadcrumb and no GPS-path heartbeat is written. Compensating control (real, verified): the driver UI independently polls POST /api/driver/heartbeat (src/app/driver/live-tracking/page.tsx:875 → api/driver/heartbeat/route.ts:12 → extend_trip_lock), which does not touch the GPS guard, so the trip lock survives. Residual: up to 100% location-trail loss for the outage duration, and bus_locations goes stale (parent/admin live map freezes).
#12 — WS pub/sub subscriptions are never registered if Redis is down at boot (RELIABILITY, P3, HIGH)
RedisPubSub.subscribe returns without doing anything when !redisClient.isReady() (server/redis-pubsub.ts:11-15), so initRedisBroadcastRelay's two subscribe calls — the cross-node broadcast channel and role_invalidate (server/redis-broadcast.ts:128) — never populate pubSubHandlers. initSubSocket on reconnect re-subscribes only channels already in that map (server/redis-client.ts:111-113), so the node stays deaf for the rest of the process lifetime. Even after successful registration, publish silently drops when disconnected (redis-client.ts:256-259, redis-pubsub.ts:5-9) — no error, .catch never fires. Worst case: GPS/trip/waiting-flag events don't reach other nodes' clients, and role_invalidate never closes sockets on those nodes, so a revoked user keeps an open privileged WS session (bounded only by the 90s tokenAuthCache TTL on re-auth — but the socket is never closed). Fix: register handlers unconditionally and let initSubSocket perform the SUBSCRIBE.
#B — Approval capacity double-count; __seatCompensation is dead code (DATA INTEGRITY, P3, MEDIUM-HIGH)
approve() calls identity_activate_student_with_capacity (src/domains/application/services/application.service.ts:462) and only afterwards finalize_application_approval (:511). Inside the RPC, the bus row is locked and morning_load/evening_load incremented (SQL:775-806) before identity_activate_student (SQL:809), and there is no check that this uid already holds a seat — already_activated is returned but the increment stands (SQL:816-825). If finalize fails (lease stolen/expired, SQL:910-926,961-967) the catch block reads app.__seatCompensation — a property assigned nowhere in src/ (single grep hit, read-only at :537) — so nothing is released, and then release_application_lock (:545) clears the lease so the retry's CAS succeeds and the increment runs a second time. Result: 2+ counted seats for 1 student → false CAPACITY_FULL → denial of seat. Compensating controls: adminReconcileBusLoads recount (src/lib/services/admin-reconcile-bus-loads.ts:87) run by cron/integrity-sweep (vercel.json:36-39, integrity-sweep/route.ts:39-47) — but gated on isSeatReleaseAtSoftBlockEnabled() and only daily at 03:17. Contrast: approve_renewal_with_seat does this correctly — it re-checks the lease inside the same tx and compensates student and bus (SQL:1819-1836).
#5 — Cron soft-block TOCTOU vs a renewal landing in the window (DATA INTEGRITY, P3, HIGH)
cleanup-expired-students re-reads the student (route.ts:227), evaluates validUntil in JS (:239), then invokes soft_block_student_with_seat_release (:250). The RPC locks the student and validates only status = 'active' (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1588-1598) — it never re-reads valid_until/soft_block. A renewal approval committing between :239 and :250 (student goes active with a fresh valid_until) is therefore soft-blocked and its seat released. Window ≈ one DB round-trip but the cron touches every expired student in a loop. Compensating control: adminReconcileBusLoads at route.ts:397 restores load counters — it does not restore status, so a student can be left soft-blocked with a valid pass until an admin notices. Same root cause as #14.
#1 — Process-local deadline/system config caches break cross-node agreement (CONSISTENCY, P3, MEDIUM)
calendar.repository.ts keeps activeConfigCache for 5 min in module scope (:31-40, populated :178); invalidateActiveConfigCache() sets it to null (:34-35) and is called only from the update path (:209) — there is no Redis/broadcast equivalent, unlike invalidateCachedRole which does publish (role-cache.ts:138). A second 5-min process-local cache exists for system config (domains/admin/services/config.service.ts:89,129). Consequence: after an admin moves the academic-year/deadline/soft-block anchor, node A computes new validUntil/blockDates while node B (running approve, a renewal, or the expiry cron) still uses the old ones for up to 5 min — identical inputs, two different results, self-healing only when the TTL lapses.
#D — Lock-order inversion between approval and reassignment (RELIABILITY, P3, MEDIUM)
Three RPCs take the same two rows in opposite orders: identity_activate_student_with_capacity locks bus (SQL:775-779 FOR UPDATE) and only then activates the student (:809), while approve_renewal_with_seat locks student (SQL:1739-1744) then bus (:1750-1755), and reassign_students_atomically locks student then buses (SQL:2167+). Concurrent approval ∥ reassignment (or two reassignments with inverse from/to ordering) → PostgreSQL detects a cycle (40P01), one transaction aborts whole. Atomic rollback means no capacity corruption; the observable failure is a spurious 500 and a moderator retry. Secondary: the rollback snapshot's bus recount runs from student_profiles before any bus row lock is held (SQL:2422-2435), so a concurrent write can be lost (P4).
#E — Moderator permission cache has no cross-node invalidation (CONSISTENCY, P3, MEDIUM)
invalidateModeratorPermissionCache(uid) only does permissionCache.delete(uid) on a module-level Map with a 60 s TTL (src/lib/security/moderator-permissions.ts:14-15,52-54). It is invoked from moderators/[id]/permissions/route.ts:84 and [id]/status/route.ts:48 — local process only. Role-level revocation is propagated via invalidateCachedRole → Redis (role-cache.ts:138) and socket teardown (redis-broadcast.ts:128-156), and moderators/[id]/update correctly blocks role|permissions|status (update/route.ts:16-18), so no privilege escalation path exists — but a revoked applications.canApprove/canReject keeps being enforced on other nodes for up to 60 s.
#A/B/C/D/E — cross-references
#A and #C are the two P2s; #B and #D share the seat-allocation subsystem; #E is bounded by the 60 s TTL. The two matrix-only YES defects (#8 orphan bus_locations, #18 rate-limiter fallback/eviction) are P4/config-dependent and are subsumed by A (spoofing) and 11 (Redis outage).
3. SYSTEMIC OBSERVATIONS
1. Idempotency primitives exist but are unused: processed_payments_acquire / release / cleanup / record_processed_payment are defined (COMPLETE_SCHEMA.sql:1051-1076) and referenced only by a comment (src/lib/notification-expiry.ts:16). The working control is payments.payment_id UNIQUE + explicit 23505 mapping.
2. The strongest invariant in the codebase is the approval CAS lease (SQL:844-851); its weakness is that the fresh approval path does not participate in the lease for its capacity increment.
3. Redis is a silent-failure dependency in 4 places (redis-pubsub.ts:5-21, redis-client.ts:256-259, rate-limiter fallback, GPS fail-closed) — three degrade silently, one fails loudly.
4. Two caches that should be distributed are process-local by design: deadline/system config (5 min) and moderator permissions (60 s).
5. Cron cadence drift: cleanup-stale-locks code says "every minute", vercel.json says daily; integrity-sweep self-heal is gated behind a feature flag.

DUP-01 · SEVERITY MEDIUM · CONFIDENCE HIGH · CLASSIFICATION: CONSISTENCY DEFECT

INT-01 · SEVERITY MEDIUM · CONFIDENCE HIGH · CLASSIFICATION: SECURITY VULNERABILITY
FILES: ...\src\proxy.ts; ...\src\lib\security\api-security.ts
CODE PATH: (i) api-security.ts:202-226 accepts any *.vercel.app/*.adtu.in origin while proxy.ts:232-239 uses an exact set; (ii) api-security.ts:251-264 OPTIONS preflight computes allowOrigin = '' for a disallowed origin then allowOrigin || '*' → returns Access-Control-Allow-Origin: * with Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true (:258,:261); (iii) proxy.ts:279-282 lets a presence-only x-internal-token header (value never checked) satisfy the CSRF origin fallback; (iv) Access-Control-Allow-Headers (:260) omits X-Internal-Token/X-Load-Test-Bypass.
CURRENT BEHAVIOR: three overlapping, mutually inconsistent origin/CSRF/CORS implementations.
SCENARIO: cross-origin request from an attacker-owned https://foo.vercel.app, or a request with no Origin/Referer/Authorization but any x-internal-token value.
EFFECT: (i) defence-in-depth only — the proxy's strict check runs at proxy.ts:355 before the public-route short-circuits at :361/:366/:371, so the loose inner check is normally unreachable; (ii) spec-invalid preflight that browsers reject because *+credentials is illegal and actual responses only echo validated origins (:426-430); (iii) CSRF gate bypassed for non-browser clients only, since a browser must preflight a custom header and it is not in the allow-list.
REMEDIATION: delete the duplicate validator and re-export the proxy's; fix :258 to allowed ? origin : ''; require a real x-internal-token value or remove the branch; add X-Internal-Token explicitly if it must exist.
EVIDENCE: proxy.ts:232-239,279-282,355; api-security.ts:202-226,251-264,268-283,426-430.
